# PROMPT 05: Implement the Research agent, complete, tested and wired into the test front end

You are the builder (Claude Code) for the Mobigen project. The owner is handing you the **second agent of the Context Engine, the Research agent**, to implement completely and make work, end to end, in the existing repo and the existing static test front end. The architect (the "planning chat") wrote the design. You follow it; inside it, **use your own intelligence and judgment** (section 2).

**The one thing that matters most: the quality of the result for the next engine.** Research exists to feed **Spec** (and, through the spec, the front-end engine). Spec decides the app's features, structure (screens, navigation, roles), flows, data and rules, integrations, design, per-screen content and mock data. **Research's job is to bring, for each of those decisions, how it is usually done, how common each choice is, and what is current, with sources**, plus the details special to this idea, in a structured form Spec can use at once. A detailed, correct, well-structured, sourced, up-to-date output is the goal. When a choice trades result quality against speed, cost or tidiness, **choose the result**, within the current budget (section 11).

Repo: `~/Desktop/project/men-gen` (Mac, Python 3.13 via uv, FastAPI, Pydantic v2, PostgreSQL; use `python3` or `uv run python`, never a bare `python`).
Planning workspace (read-only for you): `~/Desktop/chats/projects/mobile-design-engine/` (the design docs).
The owner already put the provider keys in the repo's `.env`. **Never open, print, cat or grep `.env` or any file that may hold a secret** (section 3).

---

## 1. The product, in one minute (so you understand why)

Mobigen is an enterprise AI mobile-app builder. A person describes an app; the system produces a complete Expo / React Native app (TypeScript strict). The first engine is the **Context Engine**: four agents in a line, each writing one file.

1. **Compass** (built): interviews the user and writes the **Brief**; the user approves it at **Gate 1** (hash-bound, immutable after approval). Its full export (format 2) holds the Brief with stable ids (features `FTR-###`, roles `ROL-###`, apps `APP-###`, modules `MOD-###`, flows `FLW-###`, entities `ENT-###`, research requests `RR-###`), tiers (Locked, Default, Open), consumer views (including a `research` view) and an intent-level structure.
2. **Research (you build this now):** after Gate 1, searches the web for what the model cannot know (comparable products, regional providers and APIs, fees and rules as information, data shapes, local formats) and hands **Spec** one structured, verified file.
3. **Spec** (not built): one agent with three lanes (Product, Design, Screens) that turns Brief + Research into the Blueprint, Design and Screens. **It reads your output directly.**
4. **Verify and package** (not built): independent proof and the single `APP_SPEC.md`. Then the Front-end engine builds the app from it, then Backend and QA.

Why Research matters: a good small, medium or big app needs a detailed, correct spec. The user cannot supply what the world knows (what comparable apps include, which payment providers work in their city, which fields a GST invoice needs). Research brings it, **sourced and verified**, and never decides scope.

Research runs **in the foreground, visible**: it starts when the user approves Gate 1, shows live events in the front end, and Spec starts when it commits (or when the user presses Skip).

## 2. How to work

- The design (section 6 and the docs) is the **contract**. Where it is silent, **decide yourself** with good engineering judgment and record the decision (DECISIONS.md or the repo's ADR convention). Do not ask the owner what you can answer from the repo, the docs or reasoning.
- **The repo wins on facts about existing code** (names, how Compass is served, how the gateway is called). The docs win on design intent. Adapt to the repo and record a deviation with a reason.
- You may improve on the design when you have a clear, tested reason. Record every deviation in the final report.
- **Numbers in this prompt (ceilings, caps, time boxes, thresholds, slot minimums) are starting defaults, not limits.** Tune them from what you see so that the output is as complete and useful as possible. Keep them in data/config files so they are easy to change.
- **Iterate on quality.** After you have a working run, read the real outputs critically (as if you were the Spec Product lane) and improve prompts, slot schemas, query strategy and assembly until the output is genuinely rich (section 9, "Result quality bar"). Re-run what you need.
- Stop and ask only for facts only the owner can supply (for example a missing key variable name) or a conflict you cannot resolve safely.
- **Everything new lives inside `agents/research/`** (owner instruction). Outside it you may touch only: workspace and CI registration, the import-linter config, the existing static test front end (section 8), the docs copy, the guard hook if it blocks you (section 3), and, only if no public API exists, a minimal additive hook to record search costs in the existing ledger (prefer the public API; otherwise keep a research-local ledger and report it).
- **Do not hard-code any of the three example inputs.** They are examples of Compass output (section 10).
- Work in small commits, run tests as you go, and finish with a green suite, live verification and the front end working in a real browser.

## 3. Rules that stay

**Secrets.** Never read `.env`, a key or a token, and never log or print keys. Use the repo's settings loader. Add (inside `agents/research/`) a `keys_present()` that returns booleans only (for example `{"tavily": true, "serper": true}`). Expected variable names `TAVILY_API_KEY` and `SERPER_API_KEY`; if the repo's `.env.example` or settings use other names, follow them; if a key is absent, tell the owner which variable to set and finish the offline work. **Privacy and security are baseline only** (owner decision): no personal-data or name filtering, no per-program cache isolation (the cache may be shared), no heavy security testing. Do not spend effort there; spend it on the result.

**Models.** Model calls (writer Luna `gpt-6-luna`; a second "checker" model) go **only through the existing LLM gateway** with versioned call specs, mock handlers and deterministic fallbacks, like Compass. Reuse its ledger. Live use follows the repo's existing switches (`LLM_MODE=live`, `[live-mode rule phrase]=1`) for models **and** search; the owner has approved live use (section 11) and sets the switches. If they are not set when you need them, tell the owner what to set.

**Hook.** The repo's live-spend guard and formatter hooks failed for a while because bare `python` was missing, and the guard does not cover `mde_compass.live_batches`. Make the hook commands use `python3` or `uv run python`, and extend the guard to recognize `mde_research.live_*` modules, so the guard works with your live scripts instead of blocking them.

**Code.**
- Plain code controls the flow. LLM calls are single, structured, schema-validated. **No agent loops, no agent frameworks.** The only loop is the bounded round loop of section 6.5.
- Web content is untrusted data. The calls that read it (extraction, assembly, check) **have no tools**, and their output schemas have no URL, action or tool fields.
- Content lives in data files (YAML or JSON), not code: slot schemas, load weights, ceilings, the authority registry, source-class rules, label templates.
- Money: `Decimal` or integer micro-dollars. Time: timezone-aware UTC. IDs: prefixed ULIDs.
- Research must **not import other agents** (not `mde_compass`); it reads the Compass export as JSON.
- Provider adapters call Tavily and Serper by **plain HTTP with `httpx`** (async), no vendor SDKs, inside `agents/research/`, behind a `SearchPort`. Add an import-linter contract: `mde_research` may import `httpx`, the shared LLM gateway package and the standard library, nothing from other agents.
- PostgreSQL is the production store (repo migration convention) and an **in-memory store** serves dev and tests (like Compass's `--memory`), behind one interface.
- Basic web hygiene: public URLs only (skip private and local addresses); respect robots.txt; no logins or paywall bypass; a User-Agent that names the product.
- Git: follow `docs/implementation/GLOBAL_RULES.md` (or `prompts/00-global-rules.md` in the planning workspace): baseline `git status`, safety tag `pre/research-<yyyymmdd-hhmm>`, branch `feat/research-agent`, small Conventional Commits with footers `Task: prompt-05`, `Refs: AD-39..AD-52`, commit often, local only, no push, no merge into `main` without the owner. If the Brief schema with the `research` view exists only on `feat/compass-ai-interview`, start from that branch's latest commit and say so (do not merge it).
- **Backbone (Spec Graph, shared validator framework) is not built and the owner chose to build Research now.** Put Research's validators inside `agents/research/` in a small internal registry that Backbone can adopt later. Do not build Backbone.

## 5. What Research does, in plain terms

From an **approved Compass Brief** (format-2 export) it: decides how much research the idea needs from the **content of the Brief** (never from the Quick, Standard or Thorough tier); works out the Spec decisions that matter for this kind of app and idea; runs parallel workers that search the web with specific, entity-anchored queries for **how things are usually done, how common each choice is, and what is current**; reads the pages; extracts typed facts each with a verbatim quote that **code verifies**; computes prevalence and recency; checks important facts with a second model and independent sources; goes back only for what is still missing; assembles one detailed **playbook** `research.json` **indexed by the Brief's ids and organized per Spec lane**; and streams live events to the front end. It never decides scope and never marks anything Locked.

## 6. The design you implement (working summary; doc 13 is authoritative)

### 6.1 Inputs, trust, and the research load
- Input: the Compass **format-2 export** (JSON) via the API (6.9). Check `export.approved_baseline.hash == brief._hash == export.brief_hash` and refuse a run otherwise (RV-13). Do not import Compass to recompute; equality is enough (use a shared hashing utility only if one exists outside `agents/compass`).
- Use `consumer_views.research` as the main contract (idea, geography, languages, audience, references, feature_intents, glossary, research_requests, search_hints, declined, modules, entities, domain_facts, `guidance.focus`). Look up **tiers and ids in `brief`** (the view is values-only) until Compass adds tier tags. Work with and without the new Compass trust fields (`quality`, tier tags, request `origin`; prompt 04) and **derive and flag** what is missing.
- **The tier is not an input.** Ignore `guidance.size_band`, `depth`, `search_budget`, `max_requests`.
- **Research load** (data file `data/load.yaml`): `load_score = deduped features + facts of kind rule/compliance/integration_detail/nfr + 3 × apps + 5 × sensitive-data flags + 2 × named references`. Classes: **focused** < 25, **standard** 25 to 60, **deep** > 60 (calibrated on the three real runs: about 17, 31 to 41, 95; retune with evidence and report).
- **Starting defaults by class** (data file; soft, tune for result quality): searches about 20 / 40 / 80; pages read about 24 / 60 / 140; time about 3 / 6 / 10 min (if coverage is still improving, let the run continue and show "taking a bit longer"; Skip is always available); rounds 2 / 3 / 3; parallel lanes (search / extraction) 6 / 8, 8 / 12, 10 / 16; `research.json` about 12k / 20k / 32k tokens; per-lane slice about 4k / 7k / 11k. A covered topic simply stops early; these are not targets. Prefer more verified, useful detail over less; shape what Spec reads through the slices and digests.
- **Trust rules (Compass author's corrections, binding):** read values by tier (Locked = the user's verified words; Default/assumed = AI guess); "Approve all" is not review; `meta.interview.fallback_count` is **not** a full failure counter (timeouts with 0 tokens are missed; recompute from `spend.by_call` when present, and treat requests as **template text** when they show template patterns); the structure is a **hint** only (Thorough's is mostly fallback; never anchor a query on screen counts or names, never use structure counts in the load score); `later` priority does not mean defer; roles and features may be duplicated (singular and plural; two names for one thing): dedupe before building terms; do not use `open_questions` or assumptions as search seeds (junk tokens); weak Locked geography (for example a city taken from a data-residency quote) sets `geo_uncertain` (use the country plus every stated city); do not search the user's own brand name alone (its facts come from the Brief; a website or reference URL the user gave is read as a seed source; named *references* are searched, and one that cannot be told from the user's own brand is `ambiguous`); respect `declined`.
- Detect **template or thin requests** (request `origin: template` if present, repeated words like "app apps", the same audience list in most requests, empty `search_hints` with no regional entity) and **rebuild the plan from facts** (`plan_source: rebuilt`).
- Build `input_quality` (request counts by trusted/template/thin, plan_source, degraded, failing probes, structure source, geo_uncertain).

### 6.2 Jobs: organized by what Spec must decide
Eight jobs (slot schemas in data files; minimums are starting points, more verified detail is better). Every topic carries a `spec_need` and a `lane`:
- **features** (Scope, BLUE-1; lane blueprint): comparable and local apps; features by commonness (core, common, advanced, emerging); recent additions; pricing models. Slots: 3+ competitors each with 5+ features (level); norms for 8+ features with prevalence.
- **structure** (screens, navigation, roles; blueprint): the usual screen inventory per app and role; navigation patterns and when used; usual roles and what each does; module grouping. Slots: 8+ typical screens with purpose and role; 1+ navigation pattern; roles with 3+ typical tasks.
- **flows** (journeys; blueprint, screens): usual journeys per role with steps, alternates, exceptions; onboarding; empty, failed and offline cases. Slots: per main journey 4+ steps, 1+ alternate, 1+ exception.
- **data** (entities, lifecycles, rules; blueprint): usual entities, fields (type, required, values, validation), relations, statuses and transitions, business rules and formulas, KPIs; regulatory rules as information (`legal-adjacent`). Slots: per main entity 6+ fields and states where a lifecycle exists; rules with value, unit, source.
- **services** (contract, integrations; integrations): providers by need (payments, OTP and messaging, maps, telematics, identity, government portals): auth, endpoints or events, sandbox, limits, fees, regional fit, current status. Slots: per need 2+ providers with auth, events, sandbox.
- **design** (look and feel; design): category UI and UX conventions: layout and navigation patterns, onboarding, component patterns, colour and tone mood, typography and density, iconography, motion, accessibility; platform guidelines (Apple HIG, Material) as official sources; script and font needs; recent design trends. Slots: 6+ `design_convention` items across 4+ aspects; a script need when the locale is not Latin.
- **screens** (per-screen content; screens): for each usual screen pattern: sections, key components, actions, states (loading, empty, error, success), validations, microcopy conventions. Slots: patterns for the 5+ most important screens (more for deep loads, per module cluster).
- **local** (mock data and formats; mock): formats and ID patterns with validation patterns and examples; local terms and units; realistic names, price ranges and edge cases. Slots: formats where unusual; 10+ realistic examples for the main entities.
Regulations and compliance are information inside `data` and `services`, flagged `legal-adjacent`.
**Two layers per job:** the **category norm** (how apps of this kind usually do it, from several comparable sources) and the **idea-specific details** (what this Brief's unusual facts need).
**Targets come from the union** of the Spec-need areas that apply to this kind of app, Compass `research_requests`, `guidance.focus` and high-impact `domain_facts` (`focus` can lack topics that the requests cover; `focus.compliance` mixes enum values and sentences: a hint, not a list).
**Norms, not just facts.** Code computes **prevalence** for features, screens, flows, entities, integrations and design conventions: seen in n of N distinct sources or competitors, and a level `core` (most), `common`, `advanced`, `emerging` (recent and rising); fewer than 3 sources is `limited_evidence`. The model assigns normalized keys; code reconciles them.
**Latest.** Features, design and services topics prefer sources from the last 24 months (a time range on the query; `published_on` and `age_months` on every item; older than 24 months flagged `stale`); `trend` items record what changed recently. Regulations and provider facts use the newest official source.

### 6.3 Worthiness gate and query anatomy
A topic is searched if it feeds a Spec decision (`spec_need`) **and** at least one holds: it needs current or regional grounding (providers, fees, rules, trends); a norm needs evidence of how common it is (features, screens, flows, entities, design conventions); it names an entity (from the Brief or found in round 1); it involves a number or an integration's specifics. Skipped (answered from model defaults, flagged `assumed`): pure trivia that never changes a decision (a currency symbol, common date formats). **Norms for the Spec-critical areas are always worth grounding, even where the model knows them.**
Every query contains an **anchor** (named entity, discovered entity, or a domain noun from the user's words), a **place** (Locked geography, or country plus every stated city when `geo_uncertain`) and an **intent word** (features, screens, flow, fees, rules, API, sandbox, limits, design, UX). A category-only query is poor, so do not emit one. Short first (about 4 to 12 words), at most about 25 words, no URLs or key-like strings.
Names from model memory appear **only in discovery queries** and are labelled `entity_from_model`; they are never facts. Seeds from Locked facts first; Default-tier seeds give `assumption_derived` queries, and results never raise a tier.

### 6.4 The per-topic worker (a fixed pipeline, one per topic, in parallel, isolated context)
1. **Query writer** (model, small): sees only its topic, slot schema, anchors and trust flags → 2 or 3 variants: *literal* (named entity quoted, exact match), *descriptive*, *local-language* (when the region's language is not English; Hinglish is searched in English with transliterated terms).
2. **Search** all variants in parallel through `SearchPort` (with a 24-month time range where recency matters); **reciprocal rank fusion** (k=60) and canonical-URL dedupe; source-class ranking and the authority registry (official domains preferred for rules).
3. **Triage:** code score (source class, snippet relevance, domain diversity, at most about 2 per domain); one batched model call only if more than about 12 candidates; keep the best 3 to 5 pages.
4. **Read:** provider page text if present, else `FetchPort`; **passage selection** (BM25 windows around slot terms), not blind truncation.
5. **Extract** (model, no tools), in parallel per page, into the slot-typed record: paraphrase ≤ 40 words, verbatim quote ≤ 25 words, `impact` normal or high, a normalized key (feature, screen, flow, entity, integration, design aspect) and the page's date. Extract as much useful, specific, structured detail as the page supports (screen sections and states, entity fields and enums, steps and exceptions, provider capabilities, numbers), not vague summaries.
6. **Verify (code):** the quote must be a substring of the stored page text after NFKC, whitespace, case, quote-mark, dash and zero-width normalization (RV-02); source URL and date in the fetch log (RV-03). One retry "quote exactly"; else drop. Update slot fill; emit events.
Workers return only verified, typed items. No global barrier between topics (pipeline them).

### 6.5 Rounds, coverage audit, stopping
- Round 1 covers every surviving topic. Then a **coverage audit** (code): a required slot is filled by ≥ 1 verified item (`impact: high` needs 2 independent domains or is flagged `single-source`). A topic with all required slots filled is **covered** and spends no more.
- A **gap analyst** (model) proposes follow-up queries for unfilled required slots and for entities discovered in round 1 that match a slot (including one query restricted to the entity's own domain to read its official pages). Follow-ups must differ from earlier queries; after a couple of distinct follow-ups the topic is `not_found` (a gap listing the attempted queries). That keeps the loop from searching forever for something that does not exist.
- Stop when all topics are covered or `not_found`, or a round adds very little (fewer than about 10% of the remaining required slots), or the defaults in 6.1 are reached and coverage has stopped improving, or Skip. At the end, finish extraction of pages already read and commit (`partial` when something was left unfinished).
- **Checker** (second model, batched): `impact: high` items only; sees claim + quote only; `supported` / `partial` / `not_supported`. Code checks stay primary (quote substring, two independent domains, official first; forum, blog and SEO pages cannot support high-impact claims).
- Per-call timeouts (search about 8 s, page fetch about 10 s, model about 45 s, one retry; tune). **A timeout or a 0-token model call counts as a failure** (`budget.failures`) and shows as a `warning` event. On HTTP 429: back off, tell the user, continue.

### 6.6 Assembly (for Spec)
Code builds: the indexes `by_feature`, `by_role`, `by_entity`, `by_flow`, `by_integration` keyed by **Brief ids** (every `applies_to` id must exist in the Brief or be `NEW`; RV-16); **`norms[]`** (per normalized key: `seen_in` n of N, level core/common/advanced/emerging/limited_evidence, recency, `matches_brief` by fuzzy match); `suggestions` (features, screens, fields, states, validations not in the Brief; always `undecided`, never scope); `confirm[]` (numbers and legal-adjacent facts for the owner to confirm); `defaults_proposed[]` mapped to builder needs (BN-02, 04, 05, 07, 10); `gaps[]`; `conflicts[]`; and **digests**: a compact, code-rendered, table-style text per Spec lane (`blueprint`, `integrations`, `design`, `screens`, `mock`, `verify`), for example the top norms with prevalence and level, the typical screen inventory, entity field tables, provider comparison tables. **One** model call (no tools) selects and orders items per lane and writes `use_note` (≤ 15 words); **it writes no new facts** (RV-05). Do not write sections in parallel.

### 6.7 Output: `research.json` (schema `research.v4`)
Every item: `id` (`RI-###`), `job`, `spec_need`, `kind`, `label` (≤ 8 words), `claim` (≤ 40 words), `use_note` (≤ 15 words), `slot`, `impact`, `confidence`, `tier` (**`default` or `open` only, never `locked`**), `flags` (`single-source`, `conflict`, `stale`, `needs-confirm`, `legal-adjacent`, `entity_from_model`, `assumption_derived`, `geo_uncertain`, `limited_evidence`, `withdrawn`), `applies_to[]` (`{kind: FTR|ROL|APP|MOD|ENT|FLW|INT|NEW, id, relation: expects|constrains|refines|alternative|suggests}`), `sources[]` (id, domain, `source_class` official|store|help|press|blog|forum|seo, url, retrieved_on, published_on), `independent_domains`, `prevalence` (`{seen_in, of, level}`), `recency` (`{newest_source, age_months, trend}`), `request_ids`, `target_id`, and `data` by `kind`: `competitor`, `reference_check`, `screen_pattern`, `navigation_pattern`, `role_pattern`, `workflow`, `entity_pattern`, `lifecycle`, `rule`, `service_option`, `compliance_note`, `design_convention`, `script_need`, `local_format`, `data_hint`, `trend` (fields in doc 13 §9.2).
Top level: `schema`, `program_id`, `brief_hash`, `status` (complete|partial|failed|skipped), `profile` (load_score, load_class, rounds), `input_quality`, `budget` (searches, pages, seconds, usd, failures), `coverage`, the five indexes, `items`, `norms`, `suggestions`, `defaults_proposed`, `confirm`, `gaps`, `conflicts`, `slices` (item ids + digest per lane), `claims_used` (empty; Spec fills), `dropped` counts. Quotes stay in the internal `research_evidence.json` (fetch log, stored page text, extractions, quote ids, check results, full query log, event log), never read downstream. Write JSON Schemas for `research.v4` and `research.event.v1` in `agents/research/contracts/` and validate against them. Short strings, enums, no markdown inside fields: **AI-friendly and front-end-renderable**.

### 6.8 Live events (`research.event.v1`)
SSE with `Last-Event-ID` resume; every event stored (replay after reload). Event: `id`, `seq`, `ts`, `run_id`, `type`, `target_id`, `round`, `phase`, `label` (user-facing text **built by code from templates and Compass's `shown_to_user_as` strings, never written by a model at run time**), `detail`, `counters` (searches_used, searches_planned, pages_read, facts_verified, targets_done, targets_total, elapsed_s, eta_s). Types: `run.started`, `plan.ready`, `target.started`, `target.covered`, `target.not_found`, `target.cancelled`, `query.issued`, `search.results`, `page.reading`, `page.read`, `page.skipped` (no_content, paywall, blocked), `fact.found` (unchecked preview), `fact.verified`, `fact.rejected`, `entity.discovered`, `round.completed`, `gap.detected`, `warning`, `run.finished`, `run.skipped`, `run.failed`. Events carry domains and titles, never page text or quotes. Throttle `page.*` events. The same stream serves the test front end now and the full front end later.

### 6.9 API (FastAPI; `create_app()` and `router`)
`POST /research/runs` (body: `program_id` and the Compass `brief_export`; one run per program at a time), `GET /research/runs/{id}`, `GET /research/runs/{id}/events` (SSE, resumable), `POST /research/runs/{id}/skip`, `POST /research/runs/{id}/targets/{target_id}/cancel`, `GET /research/programs/{id}/latest` (`research.json`), `GET /research/programs/{id}/review` (items with links and dates for the findings page), `POST /research/runs/{id}/items/{item_id}/reject`. Dev server `python -m mde_research --memory --port 8120` (CORS for the test front end). CLI `python -m mde_research.run --export <path> [--mock|--live]` for headless runs. A delta re-run for a later Brief version keyed by Compass `_section_hashes` is a stretch goal after everything else works.

### 6.10 Providers and ports
- **SearchPort** adapters (plain `httpx`): **Tavily** (`POST /search`: `query`, `search_depth` basic, `topic` general, `country` lowercase country name (only for `general`), `language` ISO 639-1 with `filter_by_language`, `include_domains` with `include_domains_mode` prefer or restrict, `exact_match`, `time_range`, `max_results` ≤ 20, `include_raw_content` for page text) and **Serper** (Google results: `POST https://google.serper.dev/search`, key in the `X-API-KEY` header, body `q`, `gl` country, `hl` language, `num`, optional `tbs`; links and snippets only). **Verify both against the official documentation before coding** and record URL and date in the report; do not trust this summary. Normalize to `SearchHit {url, title, snippet, content?, published?, rank, provider_score}`; support domain preference, domain restriction (Serper: `site:`), exact match, time range, country and language; report which locale parameters were applied (`locale: weak` when not). Log cost per call. **Brave is deferred: do not build it**, but keep the port generic.
- **FetchPort** (in `agents/research/`): trafilatura main-text extraction; PDF text layer (no OCR); the web-hygiene rules of section 3; content hash. JavaScript-only pages: skip and mark `no_content`. No headless browser. Use Tavily's page text when present, `FetchPort` otherwise (always for Serper).
- **Models:** writer = Luna for plan, queries, extraction, gap analysis and assembly (planning and assembly at higher effort, extraction low); **checker** = a second model through the gateway (a different model from the writer; configurable; mock in tests). Register call specs RSCH-1 (plan), RSCH-1b (query writer), RSCH-1c (gap analyst), RSCH-2 (extract), RSCH-3 (assemble), RSCH-4 (check) with `.jinja` prompt templates, strict JSON schemas, examples, mock outputs and deterministic fallbacks (a fallback is a counted, flagged failure).
- **Source classes and the authority registry:** `data/authority_registry.yaml` (India seed: official domains for GST and e-way bill, RBI, NPCI/UPI, MeitY/DPDP, transport/Parivahan, company registrar and similar, organized by country and topic so other countries can be added). Classify sources by registry, TLD patterns (`.gov`, `.gov.in`, `.nic.in`), documentation subdomains, app-store domains, and heuristics for `seo`, `blog`, `forum`.
- **Caching:** per program, keyed by normalized query + country + language + freshness bucket; never shared across programs. TTL 14 days general, 2 days prices and fees.

## 7. Traps in the real data (design for them; do not hard-code them)
- Quick's `seed_research` timed out (60 s, 0 tokens) yet `fallback_count` is 0; its requests are template text ("yoga studio app apps"; the same audience list in every request) and `search_hints` are empty → detect and rebuild.
- Two of three runs contain the user's own brand name (a studio name; a company name). Not a search term on its own; a "reference" equal to it is `ambiguous` or `not_found`.
- Thorough: roles duplicated (singular and plural; groups and specific roles), "Fleet maintenance screens" as both a role and an app, 26 of 30 features `nice`, junk `open_questions` ("FASTag", "pilot_small"), weak Locked geography (a city taken from a data-residency quote while six cities are stated), structure mostly fallback (132 screens), two facts with empty quotes, a 4 MB file.
- Standard: `focus` lacks compliance and integration topics that its requests cover; `meta.degraded` is true and the Brief was still approved; duplicate features ("Online payment" / "Pay for orders online").
- 20 requests (Thorough) are more than one-to-one searching should cover: **merge by entity and priority**; topics that do not run become visible gaps.
- Languages: Kannada, Marathi, Hindi, English, Hinglish sent as `hi`: Unicode normalization for quote checks; flag mismatches, never guess.

## 8. The existing static test front end (integrate it; it must work)
There is already a static test front end for Compass (served by the Compass dev server; find it in the repo, probably `apps/web` or inside `agents/compass`). Integrate Research into it **without a build step** if it is static, matching its look and conventions:
1. **After Gate 1 approval** ("Approve all and continue"), automatically start a Research run for that session: fetch the session's format-2 export from the Compass server (`GET /v1/compass/sessions/{id}/export`) and `POST` it to the Research server. A `research_base_url` setting (default `http://127.0.0.1:8120`); handle CORS.
2. **"Researching your idea" screen:** subscribe to the SSE stream; show the plan (topics with user-facing labels, plus topics skipped as "we already know this"), a live feed (queries issued, domains found, pages being read, facts moving from "checking" to "checked", companies and providers discovered), coverage progress, counters, elapsed and remaining time, the research depth in plain words; a **Skip research** button and a **cancel** control per topic. Reconnect with `Last-Event-ID` after a reload.
3. **"What we found" screen** (on `run.finished`): a **Playbook** view per Spec lane (blueprint, integrations, design, screens, mock) and tabs per job (features, structure, flows, data, services, design, screens, local, plus gaps and conflicts); a **Norms** view (what is usual, with prevalence "seen in n of N" and level core, common, advanced, emerging, and the newest source date); each item shows label, claim, source domain, date, link, confidence, flags; suggested features as toggles **off by default**; a reject control (calls the reject endpoint); "Download research.json"; a collapsible raw-JSON view.
4. **Dev convenience:** a "Load a saved Compass export" control (file picker) that runs Research on any format-2 export without an interview. This is how the three example files are tried in the browser.
5. Clear states: running, partial (with reasons), skipped, failed. Plain language, no raw errors.
Verify it in a **real browser** if you have the tooling (screenshots or a scripted run); otherwise verify with HTTP and SSE and say exactly what you checked. It must work end to end.

## 9. Testing, the result quality bar, and acceptance

**Result quality bar (the main test).** After live runs, review the outputs as the Spec lanes would. Using only the lane digests you should be able to: **blueprint**: list what apps of this kind usually include with how common each feature is, the typical screens and navigation, the main journeys with steps and exceptions, the main entities with fields, types, enums, validations and states, and the rules with numbers; **integrations**: compare providers per need with auth, events, sandbox, limits and fees; **design**: state the category's UI and UX conventions (layout, navigation, onboarding, components, colour and tone mood, typography and density, accessibility, script needs) and recent trends; **screens**: for each key screen, list sections, components, actions and the loading, empty, error and success states, plus validations; **mock**: produce realistic local sample data and know the formats; and know what needs the owner's confirmation. Items must be specific to the idea (no generic filler), correctly attached to the Brief's ids, sourced, dated, and quantified (prevalence and recency). If the output is shallow, generic, stale, thin or badly attached to ids, fix the planner, slot schemas, prompts, query strategy or assembly and run again. Report your own critical assessment with examples.

**Offline (no keys, no network; mock/replay; the default for CI)**
- Unit and property tests for every module: load score and classes (the three fixtures give focused, standard and deep **without reading the tier**; the same Brief with a different tier value gives identical results), slot coverage, query anatomy (every emitted query has an anchor, a place and an intent word; category-only, own-brand-only, URL-bearing and over-long queries are never emitted), rank fusion, canonical URLs, passage selection, quote verification (Devanagari, Kannada, Arabic, curly quotes, dashes, zero-width characters), source classes and independent-domain counting, **prevalence and level computation, recency and `stale` flags, norm key reconciliation, `applies_to` ids against the Brief**, basic public-URL check, timeouts counted as failures, `not_found` after distinct follow-ups, "a covered topic issues no further queries", "a follow-up never repeats an earlier query".
- **Validators RV-01 to RV-17** (doc 13 §13; RV-11 is reserved and not implemented), each with a passing and a failing test.
- **Scenario tests** for the failure cases RS-01 to RS-34 (doc 13 §12): search off, provider down, 429, injected instructions in a page (ignored), time, Skip, topic cancel, tab reload (event replay), no page text, contradictions with a Locked value, name collision, own brand, thin local-language web, duplicate roles, template requests, weak geography, more topics than budget, same-vendor checker, non-ASCII quotes, and the rest.
- **Fakes:** in-memory `SearchPort` and `FetchPort` serving canned results and pages, and mock model handlers; plus **cassette-style fixtures** recorded from live runs for deterministic replay (same inputs and cassettes give the same `research.json` hash twice).
- **Generality tests (anti-hard-coding):** (a) parametrize the pipeline tests over **every** file in a fixtures folder, so a fourth export needs no code change; (b) include **at least three synthetic Briefs you write yourself** in different domains and languages (for example a dental clinic in Dubai with Arabic and English; a coffee-shop loyalty app in Nairobi with Swahili and English; a tiny single-feature utility app with almost no facts) and assert valid output; (c) a test that scans `agents/research/src/`, `data/` and prompt templates for strings that identify the example inputs (their brand names, city and place names, entity and company names; derive the list from the fixtures' `idea` and `brief.context`) and fails if any appears, **except** legitimate registry data such as official government domains; (d) the planner works on a Brief whose research requests are missing or garbage.
- **Fixtures:** a script extracts **small** fixtures from the three exports (the research view plus the id, tier and fact-kind maps from `brief`; drop histories and structure bulk) into `agents/research/tests/fixtures/`; it never edits the originals; tests do not depend on the original path.
- API tests (FastAPI test client): run lifecycle, SSE order and resume, skip, cancel, one run per program, reject endpoint, hash refusal.
- Front-end integration check (section 8).
- Full suite green in mock/replay mode; lint, type-check and import-linter pass. One suite at a time, in the foreground.

**Live (approved; section 11)**
- **B1 Bake-off, Tavily versus Serper** (`python -m mde_research.live_bakeoff`): about 40 searches built **generically** from the topics your planner derives from the three exports (no hand-written queries). Measure per provider: share of the top 5 results from the target country's domains or official sources, on-topic share (one batched cheap model judge call), distinct domains, page-text availability (Tavily raw content versus `FetchPort` on Serper hits), latency, cost per query, locale parameters applied. Write `agents/research/reports/bakeoff-<date>.md` and save cassettes. Choose the default provider: higher local share wins; within 5 points, the cheaper; keep the other as the configured alternative. If one provider fails entirely, say so and continue with the other.
- **B2 End-to-end live runs** with the chosen provider on each of the three example exports (focused, standard, deep), then on **one synthetic Brief** of your own. Save `research.json`, the event log and a one-page summary under `agents/research/examples/` (no secrets; evidence page text excluded or truncated). Record real time, searches, pages and cost against the estimates in section 11.
- **Targets (report actuals, tune toward them):** quote check passes for 100% of committed items; all validators pass; every high-impact item has two independent domains or is flagged; required slots filled ≥ 70% on the real inputs (report the slots that failed and why); at least 60% of searches contribute a used item; **at least 70% of norms backed by 3+ sources and at least 70% of design, feature and service items from the last 24 months**; every query and page appears as an event; the front end shows the run and the findings; template requests detected and rebuilt.
- If a live batch looks wrong, fix offline and re-run what you need. Quality first.

## 10. Definition of done
1. `agents/research/` implemented as specified; everything new inside it (plus the allowed registrations).
2. Offline suite green; import-linter green.
3. The test front end runs Research in a real browser (after Gate 1 and via "Load a saved Compass export"), with events, skip, cancel, findings and download working.
4. The bake-off report, the three example runs and the synthetic run completed, and the output meets the result quality bar of section 9 (with your critical assessment).
5. Provider adapters verified against official docs (URLs and dates in the report).
6. Guard hook working with the live scripts.
7. Final report (section 12), a README in `agents/research/` (how to run: offline, headless CLI, dev server, front end), and this prompt copied to `docs/build-prompts/`.

## 11. Budget (approved by the owner, 2026-11-11)
- **The owner has approved live use with a budget of USD 4.00 in total** for this task (bake-off, the three example runs, one synthetic run, and any re-runs needed for quality). **The budget can be raised later; the owner expects Research to cost more in real use.** Keep the budget as a config value (`agents/research/config`), not a constant in code.
- Rough estimates [architect judgment, to be replaced by your measurements]: bake-off about USD 0.75; focused run about 0.05 to 0.30; standard about 0.2 to 0.8; deep about 0.8 to 2.0; synthetic about 0.3. These sum to about USD 4. **Allocate between batches as you see fit.**
- **Result over thrift:** do not cut a run short or lower quality to save money. If you are approaching USD 4.00, **finish the run in progress**, do not start a new batch, report what you spent and what you would run next, and let the owner decide whether to raise the budget. Check the repo's global ledger; if it would block you, say so.
- The owner sets the [live-mode rule phrase]es (`LLM_MODE=live`, `[live-mode rule phrase]=1`) and has put the keys in `.env`. No further approval sentence is needed for live use inside this budget.

## 12. Final report (end of task)
- Branch, tags, `git log --oneline` for the task, working-tree status; exact revert commands (last commit; whole task; return to a tag without losing history).
- What was built (a short map of `agents/research/`), what was wired into the front end, and how to run everything.
- Tests: counts, which RV and RS ids pass, the generality tests, the anti-hard-coding scan result.
- Live: the bake-off table and the chosen default provider; per-run actuals (load score and class, searches, pages, seconds, USD, coverage, slots filled, failures, flags) for the three examples and the synthetic Brief; money spent versus USD 4.00.
- **Your critical assessment of output quality** against section 9's bar, with concrete examples of strong and weak items, what you changed to improve it, and what is still weak.
- Provider facts checked (URLs and dates) and anything not verified.
- Deviations from the design and why; decisions you made on your own; thresholds, slot schemas and defaults you tuned (with evidence).
- Where the design did not hold on the real inputs, and what you recommend changing in doc 13.
- Open questions for the owner, limited to facts only the owner can supply.

<result># Compass format-2 research inputs: structure report

All three files are valid format-2 exports. `export.approved_baseline.hash` equals `brief._hash` in each. I only read the three JSONs and the README, using `python3 -I` for the large file. I did not open `.env` and I wrote no files. Paths are under `~/Desktop/project/men-gen`.

## 1. Real shapes

**Top-level keys of an export:** `export`, `session`, `brief`, `consumer_views`, `structure`, `brief_versions`, `interview`, `sources`, `contributors`, `change_sets`, `spend`.
- `export` holds `format` ("compass-full-output"), `format_version` (2), `exported_at`, `session_id`, `brief_version`, `brief_hash`, `approved_baseline` and `reading_guide`.
- `approved_baseline` holds `session_id`, `version`, `hash`, `section_hashes`, `baseline_label`, `change_set_id` and `created_at`.
- `section_hashes` has 24 section keys: idea, context, audience, apps, roles, scope, modules, screens, flows, entities, references, experience, access, integrations, data, constraints, glossary, domain_facts, assumptions, open_questions, research_requests, assets, sources, declined.
- `session` has `state` APPROVED, `locale`, `user_label`, `title`, `spent_micros` and `llm_calls`.
- `sources` is `{documents, claims}`. It is empty except in the thorough file (1 document, 40 claims with `slot_hint`).

**`brief` top-level keys (33):** version, meta, idea, context, audience, apps, roles, scope, modules, screens, flows, flows, entities, references, experience, access, integrations, data, constraints, glossary, domain_facts, assumptions, open_questions, research_requests, search_hints, assets, sources, declined, readiness, ids, summary, pitch, `_hash`, `_section_hashes`.
- `_hash` is a 64-hex string. `_section_hashes` has the same 24 keys as the baseline.
- `brief.ids` is `{counters: {FTR, APP, ROL, ASM, RR, MOD, SCR, FLW, ENT, SRC, Q}, tombstones: [...], aliases: {}}`. Quick has tombstones ROL-001, ASM-004 and ASM-007.
- Slots are wrapped as `{tier, provenance[{type, ref, quote, date, contributor_id}], confidence, status, history[], value}`.
  - Tier is `"Locked" | "Default" | "Open"`.
  - Status is `empty | assumed | volunteered | confirmed | disputed`.
- Records have the same five meta fields plus their own fields. The model is `ItemMeta` in `agents/compass/src/mde_compass/domain/common.py`.
- How each id family appears:
  - **FTR-**: `scope[].id`, `domain_facts[].refs` and `flows[].features`.
  - **ROL-**: `roles[].id`, `apps[].roles`, `modules[].roles`, the keys of `entities[].access`, `flows[].role` and fact refs.
  - **APP-**: `apps[].id`, `modules[].apps` and fact refs. `scope[].apps` is empty in every file.
  - **MOD-**: `modules[].id`, `screens[].module` and `entities[].module`.
  - **FLW-**: `flows[].id`. Each flow has `role`, `steps[{screen, action}]`, `features`, `alternates` and `acceptance`.
  - **ENT-**: `entities[].id`, with `key`, `fields[]`, `states` and `access`.
  - **RR-**: `research_requests[].id`.
  - **FCT-**: `domain_facts[].id`.
  - **ASM-**, **Q-** (open questions), **SRC-** and **CLM-** also exist.

**`brief.scope[]` (features):**
- Fields: `id, name, intent, apps[], family, scope_status ("in"|"later"), priority (must|nice|later), source, size{screens,entities,integrations}, capability_tier, custom` plus the meta fields.
- `family` is null everywhere and `capability_tier` is "A" everywhere.
- `brief.scope` is a plain list of features.

**`brief.roles[]`:** `id, name_in_user_words, goal, rough_permissions[]` plus meta. `rough_permissions` is empty in all files.

**`brief.apps[]`:** `id, name, purpose, surface, audience, roles[]` plus meta.

**`brief.integrations[]`:** `{name, preference ("real_later"), notes}` plus meta. They have no id. Names are generic categories ("payments", "messaging").

**`brief.domain_facts[]`:**
- Fields: `id, type, kind, statement, topics[], entities[], consumers[], source, quote, refs[]` plus meta.
- `refs` is optional.
- `type` values: requirement, context, rule, role, number, preference, constraint, goal, example, exclusion.
- `kind` values: context, rule, flow, constraint, screen, integration_detail, number, preference, acceptance, entity, compliance, nfr.
- Quick and standard have tier Locked on every fact. Thorough has 149 Locked and 2 Default.
- `consumers` tags include research, blueprint, design, screens, frontend, backend, qa, verify, change_control and all.

**`brief.assumptions[]`:** `id (ASM-), claim, reason, basis (pattern|typical|structure|review|model_knowledge|assumed), impact (low|high), theme, slot_id, decision ("accepted"), what_changes`. All have tier Locked and decision accepted.

**`brief.research_requests[]` (16 fields, plain model with no meta fields):**
- Fields: `id, topic, question, decision_unlocked, consumer ("research"), entity, geography (a string), source_languages[], terms[], references[], prefer_sources[], avoid[], depth (quick|standard|deep), priority (high|medium|low), shown_to_user_as, user_added`.
- There is no `tier`, `provenance`, `origin` or `quality` on a request.
- `avoid` is empty in all 32 requests. `references` is non-empty in only 2 (thorough RR-003 and RR-010, both `["TruckSetu"]`).
- `consumer_views.research.research_requests` equals `brief.research_requests` exactly.

**`brief.references[]`:** `{label, borrow[], avoid[]}` plus meta.

**`brief.search_hints`:** `{disambiguated_names[], synonyms[], local_competitors[], languages[]}`.

**`brief.declined[]`:** `{what, reason, alternative}`. It is empty in all three files.

**`brief.glossary[]`:** `{term, synonyms[], avoid[]}` plus meta.

**`brief.data`:** a dict of slots. `data.sensitive.value` is a list of codes (`["personal_ids","financial"]`) and `data.existing_systems.value` is a list of `{name, kind, pointer}`. It exists only in thorough. The other two have `data = {}`.

**`brief.context`:** `geo.value` is a dict `{city, region, country}` with only the parts known, plus `scale_launch`. `brief.experience` has `locales`, `brand.name`, `tone_words`, `density_mode` and `devices`.

**`brief.meta`:**
- Keys: `session_id, program_id, mode, situation_profile, plan{depth, modules, question_budget, minimum_viable, interview_budget}, hypothesis, locales, schema_version, depth_profile, degraded, probes{research,blueprint,design}, probe_rounds, approvals, interview, compile_digests`.
- `meta.interview` holds `fallback_count, facts_active, coverage, stop_reason, size_band, size_source, structure_modules/screens/flows/entities/issues, budget, target, hard_cap`, and more.

| Meta value | Quick | Standard | Thorough |
|---|---|---|---|
| `degraded` | false | **true** | false |
| `fallback_count` | 0 | 0 | **9** |
| `probe_rounds` | 0 | 2 | 1 |
| `probes.design` | true | false | true |
| `readiness` (all four) | pass | pass | pass |

**`consumer_views`:** four keys, `research`, `blueprint`, `design`, `verify`.

**`consumer_views.research`** is built by `research_view()` in `agents/compass/src/mde_compass/views/consumers.py`. It carries values only, with no quotes, tiers or provenance. Keys, with examples from the quick file:
- `idea`: `{summary, pitch, category:"yoga studio app", subcategory, business_model:"Buy a pack of classes"}`.
- `geography`: `{city:"Bengaluru", region:"Indiranagar"}`.
- `languages`: `{locales:["en","kn"], meta_locales:[...], search_languages:[...]}`.
- `audience`: `{segments:[...], usage_context:["on_the_go"]}`. Standard and thorough have `traits` instead.
- `references`: `[{label, borrow, avoid}]`.
- `feature_intents`: `[{name, intent, family}]`, only scope_status "in" features, with no id and no priority.
- `glossary`: `[{term, synonyms, avoid}]`.
- `research_requests`: the full requests.
- `search_hints` and `declined`, as in the brief.
- `modules`: a list of names, for example `["Account","Classes",...]`, with no ids.
- `entities`: a list of names, for example `["Class","Booking",...]`, with no ids.
- `domain_facts`: `[{id, type, statement, topics, entities, kind?, refs?}]`. These are only facts reaching "research" or "all", deduplicated per kind and normalised statement.
- `guidance` has 10 keys:
  - `size_band` (small|medium|large)
  - `depth` (light|standard|deep)
  - `search_budget` (6/12/20)
  - `gap_fill` (bool)
  - `max_requests` (6/12/20)
  - `category`, `subcategory`, `business_model`
  - `families[]`
  - `focus`
- `guidance.focus` is a list of `{topic, about[]}`.
  - `topic` values: `landscape` (module names), `domain` (entity names), `integrations` (integration names), `compliance`, `references`, `locales`.
  - Quick has landscape, domain, integrations and locales. Standard has landscape, domain and locales. Thorough has all six.
  - Thorough's `compliance.about` mixes slot codes (`personal_ids`, `financial`) with whole fact statements.
- `structure.research` equals `consumer_views.research.guidance` in all three files.
- The research view has no `roles`, `apps`, `integrations` or `sensitive_data` keys. Roles and apps are only in `brief`, and `sensitive_data` is only in `consumer_views.blueprint`.

**`structure`:** keys are `band, source, counts, outline, slices, role_matrix, navigation, issues, research`. Each `slices[]` entry has `module, screens, roles, details[{id, statement, kind}]`.

**`interview`:** keys are `qa, facts, summary, batches`.
- `facts[]` fields: `id, type, statement, topics, entities, consumers, confidence, status ("active"), origin (stated|tap|document), source_ref, qa_card_id, claim_id, quote, unverified_quote, supersedes[], not_used_reason, effects[{slot_id, value_json, mode}], aspects[], created_at, updated_at`.
- `qa[]` entries carry the full card, including `card.origin` (planner|followup|closing|fallback|stated|document|tap).

**`spend`:** `{calls, cost_micros, by_call}`.
- `by_call` maps a call name to `{calls, latency_ms, tokens_in, tokens_out, cost_micros}`.
- Call names include `cmp.understand_turn`, `cmp.plan_questions`, `cmp.compile_brief`, `cmp.seed_research`, `cmp.probe_handoff`, `cmp.draft_module` and `cmp.read_chunk`.
- Totals: 37 calls and 26,929 micros for quick, 68 and 106,789 for standard, 100 and 488,929 for thorough.

## 2. Per-file counts and data-quality traps

| | Quick (yoga, Bengaluru) | Standard (produce, Nashik) | Thorough (freight, India) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Size | 478 KB | 1.4 MB | 4.19 MB |
| Features (raw / in-scope) | 6 / 6 | 9 / 8 (one "later") | 30 / 30 |
| Priority split | 4 must, 2 nice | 6 must, 2 nice, 1 later | 4 must, 26 nice |
| Roles | 4 | 9 | 18 |
| Apps | 1 | 4 | 6 |
| Integrations | 1 | 0 | 2 |
| Research requests (priority) | 5 (2 high, 3 medium) | 7 (4 high, 3 medium) | 20 (16 high, 4 medium) |
| Request depth | 4 standard, 1 quick | 6 standard, 1 deep | 20 deep |
| `domain_facts` in brief / in research view | 33 / 14 | 44 / 17 | 151 / 74 |
| `interview.facts` | 33 | 45 | 152 |
| Languages | 2 (en, kn) | 3 (mr, hi, en) | 2 (en, hi) |
| References | 0 | 0 | 1 |
| Geography value | city and region | city, region and country | country only |
| `structure` issues | 1 | 4 | 76 |
| `degraded` | false | true | false |

Language and geography details:
- Quick geography: `{city: Bengaluru, region: Indiranagar}`. Every request says "Bengaluru, Indiranagar".
- Standard geography: `{city: Nashik, region: Nashik district, country: India}`. Request geographies vary: "Nashik, Maharashtra, India", "Nashik district, ..." and "Maharashtra and India".
- Thorough geography: `{country: India}`, from the quote "Data stays in India". All 20 requests say "India".

**Traps in quick:**
- All 5 requests carry the template text "yoga studio app apps" (RR-001 to RR-005). The same wording appears in `entity` and `shown_to_user_as`.
- The category is "yoga studio app" and gets reused as a noun.
- The only app is APP-001 "One app for everybody", tier Default and assumed, with empty `roles`.
- Two facts are the template statements "Kind of app: yoga studio app" and "Kind of app, in detail: ...".
- Reminders and the attendance list are "nice" even though they are in scope.
- Geography is good. `references`, `search_hints.synonyms` and `local_competitors` are all empty.

**Traps in standard:**
- FTR-003 "Online payment" (nice) and FTR-006 "Pay for orders online" (must) are the same feature.
- ROL-005 "Manager side" has an empty goal and also appears as APP-004 "Manager side". It is an app name used as a role.
- ROL-006 Purchase manager, ROL-007 Owner and ROL-009 Staff are buyer sub-roles with duplicate goals.
- Three `screen_entity_access_conflict` errors are left in `structure.issues`.
- `degraded` is true because the design probe failed.
- `brand.name` is null.
- Four Open-tier `open_questions` have junk text, such as "pilot_small" used as a question text.
- Request geographies are inconsistent, and `entity` is a long phrase.
- `integrations` is empty even though payments and delivery are core. They exist only as requests.
- The user's "can wait" intent did reach `later` for FTR-008.
- Hindi and Marathi synonyms are present in `search_hints`.

**Traps in thorough:**
- Features:
  - FTR-001 to FTR-006 are umbrella features from the typed answer, and FTR-007 to FTR-030 come from the document, so the 24 document features overlap with the 6 umbrellas. Examples: "load booking and tracking" overlaps "Load booking" and "Trip tracking"; "dispatch assignment" overlaps "Dispatch management". Names never match exactly, and a fuzzy token check at Jaccard 0.4 found nothing.
  - FTR-030 "App languages" and FTR-025 and FTR-029 (audit trail and logs) are not features.
  - FTR-030 says Marathi, but `locales` is only en and hi.
- Roles:
  - 18 roles include singular and plural copies (customers/customer, dispatchers/dispatcher, drivers/driver, and similar).
  - ROL-018 "Fleet maintenance screens" is an app name used as a role.
  - 11 of 18 roles have an empty goal.
- Apps: APP-006 "Fleet maintenance screens" is not an app, and APP-003 and APP-004 have no roles.
- Requests: RR-003 "TruckSetu booking and tracking flow" researches the user's own company, and RR-010 also carries `references:["TruckSetu"]`. RR-002 is the only vague "apps and services" wording.
- Brand and references:
  - `brief.references = [TruckSetu]` at tier Default. `search_hints.local_competitors = ["TruckSetu"]`. `brand.name` is "TruckSetu".
  - The assumption "Apps you like: TruckSetu" was derived from the user's own company name, so it is the user's own brand wrongly treated as a reference and a competitor.
  - Real external references: 0.
- Facts:
  - Spurious "Kind of app: travel_hospitality" (FCT-105 and FCT-130), "payments_wallet" and "delivery_logistics" template facts. 17 of 151 facts are template-prefixed.
  - FCT-139 and FCT-140 have an empty `quote`. FCT-139 holds the real cities (Pune and the five branches) but did not reach `context.geo`.
  - FCT-144 is a geography fact mistyped as kind "compliance".
  - Five statements repeat ("Services to connect: payments"). Brief facts dedupe from 35 to 32 by statement.
- Category: `idea.category` is "delivery_logistics", a family code rather than a human label.
- Counts that disagree:
  - `meta.interview.structure_issues` is 1, but `structure.issues` has 76 entries (mostly `screen_unreachable`, plus one `must_feature_without_flow` error).
  - `fallback_count` is 9, so `structure` (132 screens) is indicative only. The README confirms this and says to re-record.
- Integrations: the brief has only generic "payments" and "messaging". GPS telematics, FASTag, e-way bill, GST e-invoice and Tally appear only as 9 `integration_detail` facts, in `data.existing_systems` and in requests.
- The 9 `integration_detail` facts are tagged `[blueprint, backend]`, not research.
- README fact counts (33, 45, 152) are `interview.facts`. `brief.domain_facts` is one fewer in standard and thorough (44 and 151).
  - FCT-012 (standard) and FCT-127 (thorough) are absent from `brief.domain_facts`.
  - FCT-127 is the glossary fact.
- Compliance quirk: the `compliance` kind exists only in the thorough run, and the research view has just 3 of the 4.

## 3. Newer trust fields

| Field | Present? |
|---|---|
| `quality` | Absent everywhere. It is not a key in any file and not in the domain model. |
| Tier tags | Present. They are on slots and records: Locked/Default/Open. |

| Request `tier`/`provenance` | Absent. Requests have `user_added` and nothing else for trust. |

- In the three files, tier counts are Quick 77 Locked and 63 Default. Standard has 253 Locked, 116 Default and 4 Open. Thorough has 588 Locked, 383 Default and 2 Open.
- Open appears only on `brief.open_questions` and is junk-filled (for example "FASTag" and "pilot_small").
- Fact trust comes from `tier`, `confidence`, `status` and `provenance` in the brief, and from `quote`, `unverified_quote` and `origin` in `interview.facts`. `consumer_views.research` strips all of these.
- `consumer_views.research.domain_facts` has no tier or quote.

## 5. Research-load score

Formula: `load = deduped features + facts of kind rule/compliance/integration_detail/nfr + 3 x apps + 5 x sensitive flags + 2 x named references`.

**Fields used:**
- Features: `brief.scope[]`, or `consumer_views.research.feature_intents`, which holds only "in" features.
- Facts: `brief.domain_facts[].kind`. The research view has fewer facts because `integration_detail` is not tagged for research.
- Apps: `brief.apps[]`. It is not in the research view.
- Sensitive flags: `len(brief.data.sensitive.value)`, which equals `consumer_views.blueprint.sensitive_data`. It is not in the research view.
- Named references: `len(brief.references)` or `consumer_views.research.references`.

| Component | Quick | Standard | Thorough |
|---|---|---|---|
| Deduped features (raw) | 6 (6) | 7 (9; merge FTR-003/006, drop "later" FTR-008) | 24 (30; drop umbrellas FTR-001..006) or 23 if FTR-030 is also dropped |
| Rule/compliance/integration_detail/nfr facts, brief | 8 (7 rule, 1 integration_detail) | 10 (10 rule) | 35 raw, 32 deduped by statement (20 rule, 9 integration_detail, 4 compliance, 2 nfr) |
| Same facts, research view | 4 | 4 | 21 |
| Apps x 3 | 1 x 3 = 3 | 4 x 3 = 12 | 6 x 3 = 18 |
| Sensitive flags x 5 | 0 | 0 | 2 x 5 = 10 |
| Named references x 2 | 0 | 0 | 1 x 2 = 2 |
| **Load, deduped, brief facts** | **17** | **29** | **86** |
| Naive (raw features, raw facts) | 17 | 31 | 95 |
| Research-view facts only, apps from brief | 13 | 23 | 75 |

- Thorough cleaned to 69 if you also drop the two bogus items (5 real apps, 0 references) and use 21 view facts. The three scores are in the ratio about 1 : 1.7 : 5.1, against search budgets of 6 : 12 : 20.
- **Computable from the export: yes, with caveats.**
  - Sensitive flags only exist when a `data.sensitive` slot was filled, which happened only in the thorough run. Quick has payments and UPI but scores 0.
  - The "deduped features" term cannot be computed mechanically. There are no exact-name duplicates, and a fuzzy token match caught only the standard payment pair.
  - The `integration_detail` count depends heavily on whether you read `brief.domain_facts` or the research view (35 against 21 in thorough).
  - The reference term gives thorough +2 for the user's own brand, so it needs the brand-name check (`brand.name` equals the reference label).
  - Apps and sensitive data live outside the research view, so the agent needs the `brief` and the blueprint view too.</result>
