# PROMPT 06 (complete version): Research agent v5, a standalone live-only app with its own front end, and every change that follows from the review

- **Task id:** `prompt-06`. **Status:** ready, not started. Written 2026-11-11 by the planning chat (sole architecture authority) after reading your build report **and your code** (read-only). **This replaces the shorter first draft of prompt 06.** If you already started from that draft, keep the work and apply the differences.
- **Design (authoritative):** [doc 13 v5](../architecture/13-research-agent-design.md) (read §0, §3.3, §4, §4.1, §4.2, §5.2, §7, §9, §10.1, §11 rules R21 to R27, §12 rows RS-35 to RS-47, §13 RV-18 to RV-23, §20) and [doc 14](../architecture/14-research-proof-plan.md) (how the owner will prove it). Decisions AD-55 to AD-60.
- **Who tests it:** the owner, afterwards, **from a front end, with live models and the live web**, using small, medium and big inputs. A person who is not an engineer must be able to do that alone. **Your job is to make it work, end to end, and leave it running.**

## 1. The one thing that matters
In this order: (1) **the research must be correct and detailed**, so that Spec does not have to guess and nothing hallucinated reaches it; (2) **the owner can test it alone**: pick an input, run it, read the result, judge it, see what it cost; (3) cost and speed, a little. When two goals conflict, take the earlier one. Be blunt in your report about what is weak.

## 2. The owner's directions for this task (binding; their words where quoted)
1. **A separate front end, separate everything.** "It will have a separate front end. It will not be integrated with the upstream engine." Own server, own UI, own port. It does not call Compass, import Compass code, or live inside the Compass console.
2. **Inputs come from the docs folder automatically.** "It should auto pick up its input from the doc folder." Default folder `docs/compass/research-inputs/` (the three real Compass exports live there: a small, a medium and a big app). Dropping a new export there must make it appear without a restart.
3. **No mock model logic.** "There will be no mock LLM call logic." The app runs live models and the live web only. No mock banner, no fake content, no silent fallback to made-up output. (Test doubles for the automated tests stay in the test tree only; section 6.1.)
4. **The only limit is money.** "There is no limit of it, and the total budget of this is $5." USD 5.00 in total for everything (the owner can restart the budget and every cent is recorded; never exceed 5.00). No per-run cap, no call-count cap, no artificial throttle of your own.
5. **The owner tests it afterwards and it must keep running.** They will give small, medium and big inputs and test from the front end. Leave the app running and give them the address and the one-line start, stop and restart commands.
6. **Earlier directions that stay:** result quality first; privacy and security are baseline for Research's own data (a light `trust` job researches them for the app, doc 13 §4.1); **regional language is not a focus**; cost and latency matter only a little; the cache stays as built (paused); inside a run everything is parallel, never batch; **nothing is "approved" until shown to work** (doc 14).

## 3. Context (so you understand why)

### 3.1 The product and the pipeline
Mobigen is an enterprise AI mobile-app builder. A person describes an app; the system produces an Expo / React Native app (TypeScript strict). The first engine, the **Context Engine**, has four agents in a line, each writing one file: **Compass** (interviews the user, writes the **Brief**, approved at Gate 1, hash-bound) → **Research** (you are changing this one) → **Spec** (Blueprint, Design and Screens lanes; not built) → **Verify and package** (not built). Then the front-end engine builds the app. Research exists to give **Spec** what the world knows and the user cannot say: how apps of this kind are usually built, how common each choice is, what is current, provider limits and fees, rules and formats, **sourced and checked**. It never decides scope; its items are Default or Open tier, never Locked.

### 3.2 What you built (your report) and what held up
Research runs foreground after Gate 1: intake and hash check, a research load from the Brief's content (focused below 25, standard 25 to 60, deep above 60; the real inputs score 17, 27 and 83), eight jobs of typed slot targets, parallel per-topic workers (query variants, Tavily and Serper through `SearchPort`, rank fusion, own `FetchPort`, passage selection, typed extraction with a verbatim quote verified by code), a coverage audit with follow-up rounds and evidence-aware stopping, a checker model, assembly into `research.v4` plus an evidence file, validators RV-01 to RV-17, resumable `research.event.v1` events, an HTTP API, a console, cassettes, determinism, and an anti-hard-coding scan. 1,136 research tests and 46 PostgreSQL tests pass. **It has never run on the real web.** Keep everything that works.

### 3.3 What the planning chat found in your code (so you can fix it; the repo wins on facts, tell me where I am wrong)
| # | Finding | Where |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Silent size cap:** items plus norms are cut to `hard.research_tokens` (16k, 28k, 44k) by deleting the lowest-prevalence items 10% at a time; niche, Brief-linked rules are `limited_evidence` by nature, so they go first. Lane slices are cut at 66, 116, 183 ids; digests sit outside the estimate | `assemble/output.py` 274-332, `data/ceilings.yaml` |
| 2 | **Checker:** high-impact atoms only, claim and quote only (never `data`), run **once after all rounds in sequential batches of 15**; a rejection cannot reopen a slot; the run clock ignores it | `engine/director.py` 482-523, `calls/check.py` |
| 3 | **No grounding:** nothing ties numbers in a claim or in `data` to the quote; no region or entity fit; `extract.system.jinja` asks for "every field with its type and allowed values" while the quote is at most 25 words (invites invented types and enums); the model's `published_on` beats the page date; claims are clipped mid-sentence at 40 words; ordered lists (steps) from two pages are concatenated; the use-note guard `novel_tokens` is weak | `engine/verify.py`, `assemble/items.py`, `core/novelty.py` |
| 4 | **Metrics that cannot fail:** `quote_check_pass` re-checks atoms that already passed; `dropped.quote_not_found` counts only retries that fail again | `metrics.py`, `engine/worker.py` 641 |
| 5 | **Norms:** integrations get one norm per item and the provider's own site counts; no syndication check in norms | `assemble/norms.py` |
| 6 | **Planner:** needs from single English words ("id", "buy", "pay", "sign"); four `when:` conditions, one used; no health, minors or several-regions detection; no security topic; no light mode (a one-feature app plans 10 topics and a 22-search pool); the design job **requires** taste items (6 conventions across 4 aspects) while platform guidelines are optional | `data/need_keywords.yaml`, `catalog/models.py` 34, `data/jobs/design.yaml` |
| 7 | **No report:** no Markdown report, evidence and validator results are not served, the console shows digests in a text block | `apps/web/console/agents/research/research.js` |
| 8 | **Operations:** no provider failover; in-process search cache; a killed run blocks its program through the unique index; startup recovery marks every active run `interrupted` for all tenants without a terminal event | `adapters/search/caching.py`, `api/app.py` 234, `store/postgres.py` 255 |
| 9 | **Validators:** only RV-04 and RV-13 refuse a commit; the rest are recorded where no API serves them | `validators/registry.py` |
| 10 | **Dead settings and doc mismatches:** `hard.targets`, `hard.lanes_*`, `triage_model_above`, `conflict_unresolved`, `run.writer_model`, `run.checker_model`, `claims_used` unused; `latest?apply_decisions=1` (D-130) does not exist; `/config` hard-codes the model name | various |
Doc 13 §20 has the full 23-row table.

### 3.4 Decisions you implement (doc 13 and 14 hold the detail)
**AD-55** three caps and no silent deletion (a main store of about 40k tokens plus an **appendix**; Spec reads the whole store when small, per-call packs when big). **AD-56** truth layer (check every item inside its topic; numbers must be in the quote; no attribute the page does not state; dates from the page; independence by content; region and entity fit; an `evidence` ladder; use rules for Spec). **AD-57** category adaptation (capability signals, a light `trust` job, light mode for tiny apps, a 3-point class margin, a reshaped design job). **AD-58** speed and cost (parallel, never batch; provider failover; cache paused). **AD-59** proof plan. **AD-60** the standalone live-only app (this prompt, parts A and B).

### 3.5 The three real inputs (what to expect)
Small: a yoga studio in Bengaluru (load 17, focused; payments and scheduling; `trust` off). Medium: a produce marketplace in Nashik (load 27, standard; payments with two-sided payouts; `trust` on). Big: freight and fleet in India (load 83, deep; 30 features; payments, tracking, government documents; `trust` always). The fixtures also hold a dental clinic (health data: `trust` on), a coffee loyalty app (payments only: `trust` off) and a tiny unit converter (light mode). Use them in your tests.

## 4. How to work
- The design is the contract; where it is silent, decide with good engineering judgment and record the decision (DECISIONS.md or the ADR convention). **The repo wins on facts about existing code.** Record every deviation with a reason in the report.
- **Rules that stay from prompt 05 sections 2 and 3:** never read `.env` or print a key (booleans only, via `keys_present()`); models only through the existing LLM gateway; plain code controls the flow, no agent loops or frameworks; web content is untrusted data (no tools on calls that read it, no URL, action or tool fields in outputs); content in data files; money as `Decimal` or integer micro-dollars; UTC time; prefixed ULIDs; Python via `uv` or `python3`, never a bare `python`; Research does not import other agents; import-linter stays green; **every new data file must pass the anti-hard-coding scan** (no example brand, city or company; generic words only; official domains only in `data/authority/`).
- **Git** (`prompts/00-global-rules.md`): baseline `git status`, safety tag `pre/research-v5-<yyyymmdd-hhmm>`, branch `feat/research-agent-v5` from the latest `feat/research-agent`, small Conventional Commits with footers `Task: prompt-06` and `Refs: AD-55..AD-60`, local only, no push, no merge. Keep the suite green at every commit.
- **Hooks:** extend the live-spend guard so it recognizes the new entry points (the app and the eval commands) without blocking tests.
- **Live use:** you do **not** set `LLM_MODE` or `[live-mode rule phrase]`. If the owner has set them in your session you may run **one** live smoke check, limited to the light-mode tiny Brief and **at most USD 0.30** (section 11). Otherwise say plainly that you did not run live.
- Re-copy the design docs from the planning workspace (`~/Desktop/chats/projects/mobile-design-engine/`, read-only) into `docs/architecture-v3/`, then read the parts listed above.

## 5. Order of work (the app must run after every phase; commit at each phase end)
- **Phase A, foundation (section 6):** RC-0 (budget restart and spend record), the live-only composition and removal of mock logic from the app path, the file-backed store, inputs discovery and preview, preflight, the run queue, the new API.
- **Phase B, front end v1 (section 7):** inputs, run, results (tolerant of both `research.v4` and `v5` fields), history, budget, preflight, guide; the test-only fake app; real-browser verification. The owner could already test live after this phase.
- **Phase C, engine P0 (section 8):** RC-1 to RC-7; update the results views; report; spot-check mode; metrics page.
- **Phase D, engine P1 (section 8):** RC-8 to RC-10; the bake-off tool and settings in the UI.
- **Phase E, hardening and handover (sections 9 to 11):** every edge case in section 9, the optimization pass (measure first), docs and runbook, full suite, browser verification, the live smoke if allowed, **leave the app running**, report.

## 6. Part A: the standalone app (back end)

### 6.1 Live-only composition, no mock logic
- `python -m mde_research_agent` (no flags needed) starts **one process, one port (default `127.0.0.1:8120`), same origin**: the API **and** the static front end. Options: `--host` (default 127.0.0.1), `--port`, `--inputs <dir>`, `--data <dir>`.
- **Remove from the app's runtime path:** `--memory`, the in-memory store, mock model handlers, the fake search and fetch ports, the "MOCK MODE" banner, any replay mode. **Move test doubles into the test tree** (`tests/support/`; if `fakes.py` and `testing.py` live in `src/`, move them and fix imports). The app's composition root must be **unable** to select a double. If the gateway's `CallSpec` contract forces a mock handler to exist inside the spec, keep the attribute but guard the composition: the app refuses to run unless `LLM_MODE=live` and `[live-mode rule phrase]=1`.
- If the switches or a provider key are missing, the app **still starts** (so the front end can explain) but every start-run action returns a plain refusal naming exactly what to set (`LLM_MODE=live`, `[live-mode rule phrase]=1`, `TAVILY_API_KEY`, `SERPER_API_KEY`; booleans only, never values). **Never fall back to canned content.**
- **Deterministic fallbacks** (deterministic query variants, default use notes, `unchecked`) are real degraded paths, not model mocks. Keep them only where they cannot invent facts, flag every use as `fallback`, count it, show it in the run view and the report. Extraction has no fallback: a failed extraction yields no items.
- **Recording is always on in the app:** every run writes its search results, pages and model calls as compressed cassettes under its run folder (developer scripts can replay them; the app cannot).

### 6.2 Store and data folder
- Data folder `RESEARCH_DATA_DIR` (default `agents/research/.runtime/`, git-ignored): `runs/<run_id>/` (`research.json`, `evidence.json.gz`, `events.jsonl`, `input.json.gz`, `cassettes/`), `spend-record.json`, `uploads/`, `spot-checks/`, `settings.json`, `logs/`.
- Add a **file-backed store** behind the existing store interface (atomic writes, append-only events, a lock for a single process, the same recovery behavior as PostgreSQL). It must pass the **same shared store contract suite** as the memory and PostgreSQL stores. PostgreSQL stays the production store (selected by configuration); **the app never uses the in-memory store.**
- Persist across restarts; a run cut off by a restart becomes `interrupted` **with a terminal event** and keeps the spend so far.

### 6.3 Inputs discovery and preview (zero cost)
- `RESEARCH_INPUTS_DIR` (default `<repo>/docs/compass/research-inputs`, **read-only for the app**; never write there). Scan `*.json` to depth 2 on every list request, cached by (path, size, mtime). Identity is the SHA-256 of the file bytes (first 12 hex); the API maps an id to a path internally, **never accepts a path from the client**; refuse symlinks that leave the folder; skip non-UTF-8 and files over 25 MB with a reason.
- A valid input is a Compass **format-2 export** whose `approved_baseline.hash`, `brief._hash` and `export.brief_hash` agree (RV-13). Otherwise list it under **skipped files** with a plain reason (`not_json`, `not_a_compass_export`, `format_not_supported`, `hash_mismatch`, `not_approved`, `too_large`, `unreadable`). Same content under two names lists once.
- **Preview** for each valid input, computed by the real intake, load, signals and the **deterministic** planner, **no model call, no web call, no spend**, under one second even for a 4 MB file: idea and category, place, languages, counts (features after dedupe, roles, apps, integrations, references, facts by kind), Compass tier and degraded flag (information only, never an input), **research load, class (Tiny, Small, Medium, Big), light mode yes or no**, signals, `trust` on or off **and why**, planned topics by job, template-request and weak-geography warnings, declined topics, and an **estimate** (searches, pages, minutes, cost with a range) from a data file seeded with your dry-run numbers plus the check-all cost and **calibrated from the spend record** once real runs exist (label it "estimate, not measured").
- Uploads (`POST`, 25 MB limit) are validated the same way and stored in `uploads/`. A run copies its input into the run folder at the start (later edits do not change a run; the UI shows "changed since this run").

### 6.4 Budget and spend record (RC-0 lives here)
- **Owner decision: USD 5.00 in total, restartable, recorded, never exceeded; no per-run cap and no call-count cap.** Remove or neutralize `RESEARCH_RUN_CAP_USD` (not binding) and keep `RESEARCH_LIVE_BUDGET_USD=5.00` as the total. **Tripwire at 4.50:** the run in progress finishes, no new run starts. **Hard stop at 5.00:** a call that would pass it is refused and the run commits `partial` with `budget_stop`.
- **Restart:** archive the current shared live-spend ledger (about USD 2.37 so far; never delete history) and start this work at 0.00. Set the repo guard so that its own margin (your report: about an 80% tripwire and a 95% effective cap) still lets **5.00 be the real ceiling**; change nothing else in the repo's cap policy; record it as a decision; tell the owner the exact environment values.
- **Spend record** (`spend-record.json`, schema versioned): periods (with archive and restart history), and one entry per live action (kind: run, bake-off, probe, ping, smoke; run id; input; USD by step: search per provider, plan, queries, extract, check, assemble, probe; running total since the restart). **Write as the run goes** (a crash must not lose spend) and reconcile with the gateway ledger at the end of the run; the record must equal the ledger delta. Expose `GET /api/budget`, `GET /api/spend`, `POST /api/budget/restart` (body `{"confirm":"RESTART"}`; refused while a run is active). Show the running total in every run summary.

### 6.5 Preflight
`GET /api/preflight` returns plain checks (id, label, ok, detail, fix): [live-mode rule phrase]es set; each provider key present (boolean); inputs folder found and how many valid files; data folder writable; store ready; budget status; UI assets present; gateway routes configured; model name in use; time. `POST /api/preflight/ping` (explicitly labelled "about USD 0.01") does one search and one tiny model call, books them, and shows the result.

### 6.6 Runs, queue, API
- **One run at a time by default** (`RESEARCH_MAX_CONCURRENT_RUNS`, default 1): further starts queue (`queued`, position shown, cancellable) and start automatically. One active run per input (a second start returns the active run). Idempotent actions.
- Keep the existing endpoints (`docs/api/research-agent.md`). Add (names are suggestions; document them): `GET /api/session` (the dev token for same-origin calls), `GET /api/inputs`, `GET /api/inputs/{id}`, `POST /api/inputs/upload`, `POST /api/runs` (`{input_id}`), `GET /api/runs`, `GET /api/budget`, `GET /api/spend`, `POST /api/budget/restart`, `GET /api/preflight`, `POST /api/preflight/ping`, `GET /api/settings`, `POST /api/settings` (`primary_provider`), `GET /research/runs/{id}/report`, `GET /research/runs/{id}/spot-check`, spot-check verdict endpoints, `GET /research/programs/{id}/latest?view=compact`, `GET /research/programs/{id}/decisions`, a paged and filtered review endpoint (`limit`, `offset`, job, lane, evidence, flags, impact, text), `GET /api/health`.
- Bind to localhost; reject state-changing requests whose `Origin` is not the app's own (a web page elsewhere must not be able to start spending); no CORS; a strict content-security policy and `X-Content-Type-Options`; structured logs to `logs/app.log` with rotation; a global error handler so one bad request or task never stops the server.

## 7. Part B: the front end
**Location:** `agents/research/ui/` (static; plain ES modules; **no build step, no framework, no CDN**); served by the app at `/`. If the repo's layout rules forbid static assets there, use a top-level `apps/research-console/` and say why. **Do not import from, extend or depend on** `apps/web/console/agents/research/` (the old Compass-console hand-over): copy any pure helper you want; leave the old module and the Compass server untouched and report its status (do not delete it without the owner's go).

**Voice:** the user is not an engineer. Plain words (topics, facts, trust label), no jargon in the interface, USD with 2 decimals (3 for tiny amounts), minutes not milliseconds. Every label comes from templates, never from a model at run time. Show **"LIVE: real models and the real web; this costs money"** permanently (no mock banner exists).

**Always-visible bar:** app name; **Preflight pill** (Ready, or Not ready with a click for the fix); **Budget bar** ("USD 1.23 of 5.00 used; tripwire 4.50"; amber at 80%, red at the tripwire); navigation (Inputs, Runs, Spot-check, Budget, Tools, Guide).

**Inputs (home):** cards sorted Tiny, Small, Medium, Big by the **computed** load (not by file name), each with the preview of section 6.3, a **Run research** button (confirm dialog: estimate against remaining budget; stronger warning above 40% of what is left; "may stop early (partial)" if the estimate exceeds what is left), **Details** (Brief summary, planned topic list, trust flags), past runs for that input, file name, modified time, short hash, a "changed since last run" badge. Auto-refresh every 10 s while visible and on focus, plus a Refresh button. Empty state: the exact folder path and how to add files. A collapsed **Skipped files** list with reasons. An optional **Add a Compass export** upload.

**Run view:** plan (topics by job with plain labels and priority; topics dropped by the gate shown as "we already know this"), live feed (queries, domains, pages being read, facts moving from "checking" to "checked" or "rejected", entities discovered), counters (searches, pages, facts verified and rejected, topics done of total, elapsed, estimate of time left), **cost so far and by step**, a one-line "what it is doing now", **Skip research** and per-topic **Cancel**, a **warnings** panel (fallback used, provider failover, rate limit waits, budget tripwire), a banner if the server restarts ("interrupted", with Re-run). Reload-safe (`Last-Event-ID`), SSE keepalive and auto-reconnect with back-off, events deduplicated by sequence number, applied in batches (one frame at a time).

**Results ("What we found"):** header with status (and what is missing if partial), load class, signals, cost, time, trust summary; tabs: **Playbook** by lane (blueprint, integrations, design, screens, mock, verify) and by job; **Norms** ("mentioned in n of N", level, newest source date); **Confirm**; **Gaps and conflicts**; **Appendix**; **Quality** (lane quality, builder-need coverage, validation results, the metrics page of RC-7, fallbacks used); **Sources** (every page with class, date, link); **Report** (rendered Markdown preview and download); **Raw JSON**. Filters (job, lane, evidence, flags, impact, text search). **Item view:** claim, data in readable form, **the best quote**, source domain, date and link, evidence badge, flags, `applies_to`, other sources, a reject control (the existing overlay). Suggestions are toggles, off by default.

**Runs (history):** status, input, class, started, duration, cost, items, lane quality; open any run; compare two runs side by side is optional.

**Spot-check (the owner's 60-fact test, doc 14 §4):** pick runs, a seed is shown, 20 items per run drawn by the strata of doc 14; **one item per card** (claim, data, quote, source link, date, evidence); six buttons (`ok`, `not relevant`, `claim not in quote`, `wrong or outdated`, `wrong place or company`, `invented detail`) with keys 1 to 6; Skip; progress; saved after every verdict, resumable; at the end the **summary against the thresholds** (supported 95% or more, ok 90% or more, no `invented detail` on a high-impact item and fewer than 3 overall), the failing job or kind, and an export (`.md` and `.json`).

**Budget:** the spend record as a table (date, what, run, cost by step, running total), the periods, **Restart budget** behind a confirmation that asks for the word RESTART and states that history is kept.

**Tools (optional, after everything else):** the provider bake-off (confirm with its cost estimate, progress, results table, "Use this provider" saved in `settings.json`). **Guide:** a collapsible step list for the owner (check Preflight; run the small input; read it; run medium and big; spot-check; read the budget).

**Quality of the front end ("optimize it"):** first paint under 1 s on localhost; the inputs list under 300 ms after load; a result with 600 items renders its first screen under 500 ms and scrolls smoothly (**server-side paging and filters, a virtualized list in the browser**, lazy tabs); filters answer under 100 ms; the whole JavaScript under about 100 KB gzipped; no layout thrash (one batched DOM write per frame); long tables and quotes wrap; `dir="auto"` for right-to-left text; works from 360 px wide to desktop; light and dark; keyboard-operable with visible focus; status changes announced (ARIA live); `prefers-reduced-motion` respected; plain-language error states for every failure (server unreachable, not ready, refused, interrupted, budget stop); remember the last input and filters in `localStorage` (wrapped in try/catch). **Security:** every string that came from a web page (claims, quotes, titles, domains, notes) is inserted as **text**, never as HTML; links are `http` or `https` only, with `rel="noopener noreferrer"` and `target="_blank"`; no inline script (CSP); no secret ever reaches the browser.

## 8. Part C: the engine changes
Paths are under `agents/research/src/mde_research_agent/`. Each change has the **problem**, the **change**, and **acceptance** (offline tests, with the doubles from the test tree).

### RC-0 (Phase A). Budget restart and spend record: section 6.4.

### RC-1 (P0). No silent drops: main store, appendix, compact view
**Change.** Remove the drop loop and `dropped.size_cap`. Replace `research_tokens` and `slice_tokens` in `data/ceilings.yaml` by `store_tokens=[REDACTED] `chunk_items: 120`, `digest_tokens`. New pure module `assemble/store.py`: per item `rank`, `tokens_est` (characters over 4, on the **compact view**) and `store` (`main` or `appendix`, with `appendix_reason`). **Protected (always main):** an item linked to a Brief id (not `NEW`) with `impact: high` or kind `rule`, `compliance_note` or `service_option`; items flagged `conflict` or `needs-confirm`; the best two of every kind. **Rank for the rest, best first:** Brief-linked, high impact, evidence (`official`, `corroborated`, `single_source`, `unverified`), then norm level as a tie-break only, then job priority, then id. If the protected set alone exceeds the limit, keep all and warn. **Compact view** (`GET .../latest?view=compact`, a deterministic projection, not a second stored file): main-store items, norms, gaps, confirm, suggestions and defaults, with **sources in one table** and `use_note` only when it differs from the kind default; `profile.store_tokens`, `profile.appendix_items`, `profile.digest_tokens`. Slices list **every main-store item of the lane**, ordered by code, split into `chunks[]` of at most `chunk_items`; digests are an index, outside `store_tokens`. RV-09 and RV-22 become **enforced**.
**Acceptance.** Above the limit, items move to the appendix and `len(items)` is unchanged; a Brief-linked high-impact `limited_evidence` rule stays while a generic `core` normal-impact item goes; atoms equal items plus merged; slices hold all main-store items of a lane and split above `chunk_items`; the compact view is smaller with the same ids; hashes stable; no `size_cap` left.

### RC-2 (P0). Check every item, inside the topic, before the audit
**Change.** Move the check into the worker (after verify): all verified atoms of the topic, in batches of 15 (`CHECK_BATCH`), **in parallel across topics** under the model semaphore, **before** `update_coverage`. New `rsch.check` v2: per item the claim, the typed `data` (compact JSON), the **passage window** (about 150 words around the quote, from the normalized page text using the offsets of quote verification), the page title and domain; output `verdict` (`supported`, `partial`, `not_supported`) and `unsupported_fields[]` (paths in `data`). Code: null unsupported fields; if the item then lacks its kind's minimum, drop it as `thin` (`unsupported_field`); `partial` adds `partial_support` and evidence `unverified`; `not_supported` rejects the atom and **the slot reopens**; a failed call or missing verdict leaves the item `unchecked` and `unverified` (never trusted, never dropped silently). Check **identical (claim, quote) pairs once**. The run clock and the budget tripwire cover checks and assembly (after the hard time ceiling no new batch starts; the rest is `unchecked`). Skip and topic cancel propagate into in-flight checks.
**Acceptance.** A `not_supported` verdict on the only item of a required slot reopens it and a follow-up query is issued; two topics' checks overlap (fake clock); every verified atom has a verdict or `unchecked`; an unsupported `type` is nulled; check cost has its own call id; determinism with random delays.

### RC-3 (P0). Ground numbers, attributes, dates; read tables; stop clipping
**Change.** New `core/numbers.py` (pure): extract numbers, amounts, percentages, ranges and dates from the claim, label and numeric `data`; normalize (Unicode digits to ASCII, separators, decimal points, currency symbols and codes, `lakh` and `crore` as written, number words one to twelve, "per cent"); each must occur in the normalized quote. **RV-18:** if absent from the quote but present in the passage, widen the stored quote to the smallest enclosing sentence (at most 40 words, flag `number_widened`); else reject `number_not_in_quote`. **Extraction prompt and kind guides:** state only what the passage states; types, required flags, allowed values, steps and states the page does not give are `null` or omitted; remove the phrase quoted in 3.3; update `domain/atoms.py` (nullable attributes, new minimums). **Dates:** the page metadata date, else a date parsed by code from the quote, title or URL; the model's date is ignored. **Claims:** cut only at a sentence end within 40 words, else reject `claim_too_long`. **Merge:** mark ordered fields (`steps`, `transitions`) in the kind schemas and never concatenate them across sources (keep the richest list, store the others as `variants`). **Use-note guard:** numbers as whole tokens, case-insensitive words, first word included; RV-05 enforced. **Page reading:** make sure the main-text extractor keeps **table cells and list items** (pricing and fee pages); detect **soft 404 and wall pages** by title, headings and the first 2 KB, not only on short pages; add fixture pages for each.
**Acceptance.** Property and golden tests: `₹5,000` against "5000"; `2.5%` against "2.5 %"; `10-15 days`; `Rs. 1.2 lakh`; Devanagari digits; "two" against 2; dates; a missing number is widened or rejected; the model's `published_on` is ignored; a 55-word claim is cut or rejected; two step lists keep one list plus `variants`; a quote from a table cell verifies; a soft-404 page is skipped; the prompt no longer asks for types or allowed values.

### RC-4 (P0). Independence by content; region and entity fit
**Change.** Apply the existing content-overlap rule (`core/sources.py`: 5-word shingles, 0.8) in norms and corroboration (RV-19), and to the quote's passage window; flag `duplicate_source`. The extractor returns `about_region` (ISO 3166-1 alpha-2, `global` or `unknown`) and `about_entity`. **RV-20:** a different specific country than the plan's place gives `other_region`; an anchored topic whose `about_entity` shares no stem gives `other_entity`. Flagged items are excluded from corroboration, norms and prevalence, get evidence `unverified`, stay (appendix-eligible, shown in the report).
**Acceptance.** The same paragraph on two domains counts once, a paraphrase twice; a regulator page for another country is flagged and raises no norm; a namesake company is flagged; both flags show in the report.

### RC-5 (P0). Readable report, evidence views, spot-check data
**Change.** `report.py`: `render_report(research, evidence) -> str`, deterministic: run header (status, class, signals, budget, time), lane quality and builder-need tables, `confirm`, conflicts, gaps with reasons, one section per job with every item (label, claim, evidence badge, flags, sources with domain, date and link, **the best quote from the evidence**, compact data, `applies_to`, a mark for owner-rejected items), the appendix, the validation summary, a query-log summary, the fallbacks used. Endpoints of 6.6 (report on demand, not stored; spot-check sample **seeded and stratified** as in doc 14 §4; review with quote and source). Put validator results in `research.json.validation` and show them.
**Acceptance.** Golden-file report test; the same seed gives the same sample; the review endpoint returns a quote per item; paging and filters work; a fake-server test covers the front end's use of them.

### RC-6 (P0). Output fields, norms, API additions, clean-up
**Change.** **Schema `research.v5`** (JSON Schema in `contracts/`; bump constants, validators and the front end): per item `evidence`, `source_language`, `about_region`, `store`, `tokens_est`, `rank`; top level `lane_quality`, `bn_coverage`, `validation`, `flagged`, the new `profile` fields. Definitions in doc 13 §9.4 (computed in code, validated by RV-23). Integration norms: one per (need, provider), merged across items, `seen_in` counts only independent third-party mentions, the provider's own docs are `evidence: official` and do not count. Events and snapshot (doc 13 §10): `plan.ready` carries `slots_required`; `fact.*` carry a stable `fact_id`; the snapshot keeps a `facts` list; `run.finished` carries stage timings and cost by stage; `GET .../decisions` (the overlay) and either implement `latest?apply_decisions=1` or correct D-130. Clean-up: use or remove every dead setting of 3.3 row 10; `/config` reads the model name from settings; fix the `data/load.yaml` comment (17, 27, 83); fix the package page.
**Acceptance.** Schema validation on all fixtures; RV-23 passes; a provider with three items gives one norm; contract tests for events and snapshot.

### RC-7 (P0). Measure the right things; replay the recorded web with live models
**Change.** Replace the near-tautological metrics (`metrics.py`, `live_runs.py`) by the doc 14 §3 definitions: **first-try and final extraction quote pass rate** (count every extraction, including `invalid` and `empty`), number-check rejections, fetch success by reason, searches contributing, slots filled per job, appendix share, lane quality, checker rates (`supported`, `partial`, `not_supported`, `unchecked`), merge rate, **stage timings and cost by stage and call kind**. One page per run, shown in the Quality tab and written next to the run. Developer scripts only: **`--replay-web --live-on-miss`** (cassettes first for search and pages, a miss goes live and is recorded, models live unless `--replay`).
**Acceptance.** A fake run produces the metrics page with every field; first-try and final rates differ when a retry recovers an item; web replay with a live-model double works and a miss without switches fails with a plain message.

### RC-8 (P1). Capability signals, `trust` job, light mode, class margin, design reshape
**Change.** Doc 13 §3.3, §4, §4.1, §4.2. `data/signals.yaml` and `plan/signals.py`: strong words (one suffices) and weak words (at least two from different features or facts), computed from feature intents, integration facts, rule and compliance facts, roles, apps, geography and the sensitive-data flags; starting signals in §4.1; extend the `Condition` list; job templates use `when:`; keep the old keyword needs only as a fallback until the signals reproduce them on the fixtures (report any difference). `data/jobs/trust.yaml` (optional slots, priority 45, kinds `compliance_note`, `rule`, `service_option`, always `legal-adjacent` and `confirm`): runs for **deep**, and for focused or standard when `health_data`, `minors`, `identity_kyc`, `multi_region` or `sensitive_data` fires, or `payments` **together with** `two_sided`; **`payments` alone must not trigger it**. Extend `data/authority/*` with data-protection regulators and the store-policy pages (generic registry data). Design job: platform guidelines (3 or more) and accessibility (2 or more) **required**, category conventions optional (3 or more across 2 or more aspects, only when a page states them); screens: 3 or more key screens required (5 or more for deep). **Light mode:** load below 12, no signal, no named reference, no integration or rule fact, no place: plan only feature norms and the screen inventory (2 variants each, soft ceiling 8 searches, about 90 s), commit `complete` with `profile.light: true` and gaps `not_worthwhile`. `class_margin: 3` in `data/load.yaml` (22 and 57). Local-language variants only in the `local` job.
**Acceptance.** The three real fixtures keep 17 focused, 27 standard, 83 deep and a score of 23 is standard; the dental clinic turns `trust` on; the yoga and coffee fixtures keep it off; the produce marketplace turns it on; a deep Brief always runs it; the tiny converter plans 2 topics; "order id" no longer triggers an identity need; anti-hard-coding scan passes; the synthetic Briefs still run.

### RC-9 (P1). Evaluation harness
**Change.** `mde_research_agent.eval` with prompts in `data/eval/` (doc 14 §5 and §6): `planted` (40 clean items and 200 corrupted variants from a recorded run: number changed, entity swapped, condition flipped, region swapped, unsupported attribute added; report what **code** catches and what the **checker** catches) and `probe` (a stand-in Spec for five tasks with and without research, whole store against pack; a judge call scores supported, `ASSUMED`, invented, and builder-need coverage). A batch API if the gateway offers one. These are commands for later; they print their cost and book it in the spend record. Tests use doubles from the test tree.
**Acceptance.** Seeded and deterministic; the corruption generator is unit-tested; costs reported.

### RC-10 (P1). Provider failover, second opinion, stale runs
**Change.** `FailoverSearch(primary, secondary)` behind `SearchPort`: on provider errors for a topic, retry on the other provider; a second opinion for topics with fewer than 3 usable results, at most 15% of searches; the primary comes from `settings.json` (the bake-off) else `providers.yaml`. **Stale-run expiry:** a run with no event for `stale_after_s` (default 600) is marked `failed: stalled` with a terminal event so its program is not blocked; startup recovery marks only runs whose last event is older than that, and emits a terminal event.
**Acceptance.** The failure drills of doc 14 §8 for provider failure, both providers failing, and a killed run.

## 9. Edge cases (each needs a test; B = also exercise it in the real browser against the test-only fake app; plus every RS row of doc 13 §12)
| Case | Expected |
|---|---|
| **Inputs folder missing or empty** | An empty state with the exact path and how to add files; no crash (B) |
| **A file that is not JSON, a README, an unrelated JSON, non-UTF-8, over 25 MB** | Listed under skipped files with a plain reason; never fatal (B) |
| **Format-1 or unknown export version; hash mismatch; not approved** | Listed as invalid with the reason; Run disabled (B) |
| **Degraded Brief, template requests, weak geography, duplicate roles, own brand** | Allowed; the preview warns; doc 13 §3.2 trust rules apply |
| **Zero features and zero facts** | Preview says there is nothing to research; a run ends `skipped: nothing_to_research` in seconds with no spend |
| **`declined` topics** | Not researched; shown as declined |
| **A 4 MB export** | Parsed once and cached; preview under 1 s |
| **File edited or replaced while listed or running** | The run uses its snapshot; the UI shows "changed since this run" |
| **Two files with the same content; path tricks, symlinks, huge names** | One card; unsafe paths refused; the client never supplies a path |
| **Right-to-left or non-English Brief text** | Rendered with `dir="auto"`; searches follow the place and language rules |
| **[live-mode rule phrase]es or a key missing** | Preflight red; Run refused naming the exact variable; no fallback content (B) |
| **Spent at or above 4.50** | No new run (explained); the running run finishes (B) |
| **A call would pass 5.00** | Refused; the run commits `partial` with `budget_stop`; the record is right (B) |
| **Estimate above the remaining budget** | A confirm dialog with the numbers (B) |
| **Spend-record write fails** | The run continues; a banner; the record is rebuilt from the ledger at the next write |
| **Budget restart** | Archives and zeroes; history kept; refused during a run; the confirm word is required (B) |
| **Crash mid-run** | Spend so far is already recorded; the run shows `interrupted` with Re-run (B) |
| **Another process spends on the shared ledger** | The Research record counts Research only; if the guard refuses earlier, the banner says why |
| **Primary provider errors or 429** | Wait as told, fail over, else `provider_down` and `partial` |
| **Model call returns invalid output or fails** | One repair, then a flagged fallback; never invented content; counted and shown |
| **Gateway outage mid-run** | Items so far kept; `partial`; a clear banner |
| **Slow site, redirect loop, huge page, image, video, zip** | Timeouts and size caps; skipped with a reason |
| **Soft 404, cookie or login wall, geo block** | Detected and skipped (RC-3) |
| **Table-heavy pricing page, long lists** | Cells and items are in the text; a quote from a cell verifies |
| **Prompt injection in a page, title, URL or claim; HTML or `javascript:` in page text** | Ignored and counted; rendered inert as text in the UI (B) |
| **Number not in quote; type or enum not in the passage; model date against page date; claim over 40 words** | RC-3 behavior |
| **Same text on two domains; page about another country or company** | RC-4 behavior |
| **Checker rejects the only item of a slot; checker call fails; identical pairs** | RC-2 behavior |
| **Two pages give different ordered lists** | One list plus `variants`, never joined |
| **Quote with curly quotes, NBSP, soft hyphens, zero-width, Devanagari digits** | NFKC folding verifies it |
| **Tiny app; score 22 to 24 or 57 to 60; `payments` alone** | Light mode; the larger class; `trust` off |
| **Skip during checks; topic cancel during checks; tripwire during checks** | Commit what is verified; unchecked items flagged; no more spend |
| **All topics `not_found` for a niche idea** | `complete` with gaps, not `failed` |
| **Store above `store_tokens`; protected set alone above it** | Appendix, nothing deleted, counts conserved; a warning and the store grows |
| **Owner rejections** | `research.json` stays immutable; the overlay shows in the views and the report |
| **Tab closed or reloaded; laptop sleeps; SSE drops; two tabs open** | The run continues; auto-reconnect; no duplicate events; both tabs agree (B) |
| **Server restarted; killed process never restarted** | `interrupted` with a terminal event; stale-run expiry frees the input |
| **Same input started twice; a queued run cancelled** | The second start returns the active run; the queue starts the next automatically (B) |
| **500 or more items; very long words, quotes and tables** | Virtualized, paged, filters under 100 ms; text wraps (B) |

## 10. Optimization (measure first, then change; report before and after)
OPT-1 **skip pages with no relevant passage** (a minimum passage score in data) so no model call is wasted; count skips. OPT-2 **extract once per page** when several topics select it in the same round (union of wanted kinds), only if measurements show duplicate extraction. OPT-3 **check identical pairs once** (RC-2). OPT-4 prompt prefixes stable for gateway caching (AD-26) where supported. OPT-5 the stage timings of RC-7 decide where latency work is worth doing; raise lanes up to the gateway limits only if rate limits allow. OPT-6 the search cache holds raw provider text: cap the size of cached entries and report memory. OPT-7 the front end budgets of section 7. OPT-8 startup: lazy imports, the preview cached by (path, size, mtime). Do **not** add a persistent page cache, a cross-run model cache or batch calls inside a run (decisions AD-58).

## 11. Testing, verification, handover
- **Automated:** the full offline suite green (research, PostgreSQL integration, the file-store contract, console and Compass API tests, `just layers`, ruff, pyright for `agents/research`; report the repo-wide red that predates this separately). Tests per RC acceptance and per row of section 9. Node tests for the front end's pure helpers (event reducer, formatters, sanitizers, virtual list math, filters). The generality and anti-hard-coding tests stay green; update golden hashes consciously and say so.
- **Test-only fake app** (`tests/e2e/fake_app.py`, never importable from the app): the same server with doubles and a loud "TEST FAKE" banner, used to drive **every screen and every (B) row in a real browser** (Chrome tooling; screenshots at 360 px and desktop, light and dark; include an XSS fixture page). Say exactly what you checked.
- **Live smoke (only if the owner set the switches in your session):** one light-mode run of the tiny Brief through the real app, **at most USD 0.30**, booked in the spend record; confirm live search and live models, the events, the report and the cost line; read the output as Spec would. If the switches are absent, do not run live and say so.
- **Definition of done:** parts A, B and C complete; every RC acceptance and (B) edge case verified; docs updated (`docs/api/research-agent.md`, `docs/runbooks/research-agent.md` with a "Test it from the front end" section the owner can follow, the package page, an ADR addendum, DECISIONS); the guard hook updated; **the app left running** (start it only if the [live-mode rule phrase]es are in your environment; otherwise leave it stopped and give the one-line detached start the owner types with their switches). Provide `scripts/research_app` (or `just` recipes) for `start` (detached, PID file, log), `stop`, `status`, `logs`, `restart`, and print the URL.

## 12. Not in scope
Compass and other agents (leave the old Compass-console hand-over untouched and report it); Spec and Verify; Backbone; the legacy `mde_research`; `.env`; setting the [live-mode rule phrase]es; merging or pushing; changing the three real Compass exports; any caching or batching decision other than those above.

## 13. Final report
Branch, tags, commits, tests with counts; per phase and per RC what was built and what was not; deviations and why; where you think this prompt or the design is wrong; the **address, commands and data locations**; the **budget status** (restart done, exact environment values, spend record location, tripwire); the metrics page and a report sample on a fixture (and the live smoke result if run); a printed `research.v5` item for each evidence level; what is still weak, bluntly; revert commands per the global rules.
**The owner's acceptance checklist (copy it into the runbook):** (1) open the address, Preflight is Ready; (2) the three inputs show as Small, Medium, Big with a preview and an estimate; (3) run the small one and watch it live; (4) read the results and the Quality tab; download the report; (5) run medium, then big; (6) do the spot-check; (7) the budget bar and the spend record agree with the runs; (8) close the tab during a run and come back; (9) drop a new export in the docs folder and see it appear; (10) a bad file shows a reason.

## 13 §4.1 capability signals and the trust job
- **Mechanism:** signals are computed by code from feature intents, integration facts, rule and compliance facts, roles, apps, geography and the sensitive-data flags. Each signal has strong words (one is enough) and weak words (at least 2, from different features or facts). The word lists live in data, and the builder tunes them.
- **Starting signals [J]:** `payments`, `messaging`, `maps_location`, `tracking`, `identity_kyc`, `government_documents` (a tax and rules topic, not a trust trigger), `accounting`, `scheduling`, `two_sided`, `media_upload`, `health_data`, `minors`, `multi_region`, `non_latin_locale`, `many_roles` (5 or more), `multi_app` (2 or more), `sensitive_data` (the Compass flags).
- **No strong or weak word lists are written in the docs.** The implementer must author them. The anti-hard-coding scan applies: generic words only.
- **Trust runs when:**
  - the class is **deep**, always; or
  - for focused or standard, `health_data`, `minors`, `identity_kyc`, `multi_region` or `sensitive_data` fires; or
  - `payments` fires **together with** `two_sided`.
- `payments` alone does not trigger it (OI-21). A content trigger overrides size.
- **Trust job properties:** priority 45; kinds `compliance_note`, `rule`, `service_option`; always `legal-adjacent` and `confirm`; at most 3 countries.
- **Inconsistency inside doc 13:** the job table says all slots are optional "except the store disclosure when the job runs". The prose and prompt RC-8 say optional slots only.

## 13 §4.2 light mode
- **Conditions:** load below about 12 AND no signal AND no named reference AND no integration or rule fact AND no place.
- **Plan:** only the feature-norms topic and the screen-inventory topic, 2 variants each, soft ceiling 8 searches, about 90 s.
- **Commit:** `complete` with `profile.light: true`. Skipped topics become gaps `not_worthwhile`.
- **Doc 14 tripwire:** tighten the rule if a tiny app uses more than 3 searches or 90 s.
- **Class margin:** `class_margin: 3` in `data/load.yaml`. A score within 3 below a boundary uses the larger class, so the boundaries become 22 and 57. A score of 23 is standard.
- **Load formula:** deduped features, plus facts of kind rule, compliance, integration_detail or nfr, plus 3 × apps, plus 5 × sensitive flags, plus 2 × named references. Classes: focused below 25, standard 25 to 60, deep above 60.
- **Real loads:** 17, 27 and 83. Only 17 is unit-tested. The §6.6 paper-test table still says "31 to 41" and "95", which is stale.

## 13 §7.2 the three caps
1. **Loop cap (built):**
   - `min_round_gain: 0.10`.
   - At most 2 follow-ups per target, soft 2 and hard 3.
   - Then `not_found`.
2. **Store cap (proposed):**
   - `store_tokens` = **40,000** estimated tokens for every class.
   - The measure is the compact view, estimated as characters over 4.
   - Overflow gets `store: appendix` plus a reason.
   - **Protected, in order:** Brief-linked and high impact; flagged `conflict` or `needs-confirm`; evidence rank (official &gt; corroborated &gt; single_source &gt; unverified); prevalence as tie-break only; the best 2 of every kind always stay.
   - If the protected set alone exceeds the limit, warn and let the store grow.
   - **Prompt 06 RC-1 differs:** protected includes Brief-linked items that are high impact **or** kind `rule`, `compliance_note` or `service_option`. It adds job priority, then id, as final tie-breaks.
   - **Expected sizes [J]:** focused 10k to 15k, standard 25k to 40k, deep at the limit plus an appendix.
3. **Pack cap (Spec side):**
   - The routing threshold defaults to 40k.
   - At or under it, the whole main store goes to every Spec call as a cached prefix, with digests left out.
   - Above it, Spec builds a pack per call from a Need Card: records matching the Brief ids, plus one hop along `applies_to`, plus lane essentials, plus a title-only index.
   - An oversize pack is split into more calls, never truncated.
- **`chunk_items`** = 120 per lane chunk. `digest_tokens` is a profile field only. Digests sit outside `store_tokens`.
- **Replaced settings:** `research_tokens` (16k, 28k, 44k) and `slice_tokens`.
- **Dead settings to use or remove:** `hard.targets`, `hard.lanes_*`, `triage_model_above`. Note that §20 row 13 says `lanes_search` and `lanes_extract` ARE used, so there is a mild inconsistency.

## 13 §9.2 to §9.4 v5 output shape
**Item fields:**
- New: `evidence`, `source_language`, `about_region`, `store`, `tokens_est`, `rank`.
- `appendix_reason` appears in prompt RC-1 only.
- Limits: `label` ≤8 words, `claim` ≤40, `use_note` ≤15.
- Quotes stay in `research_evidence.json`, not in items.

**Top-level additions:**
- `profile` = {`load_score`, `load_class`, `rounds`, `light`, `signals[]`, `store_tokens`, `appendix_items`, `digest_tokens`}.
- `lane_quality` has lanes blueprint, integrations, design, screens, mock and verify.
- `bn_coverage` covers BN-02, 03, 04, 05, 07, 08, 09 and 10.
- `validation` = {`errors`, `warnings`, `by_rule`}.
- `dropped` keys: `quote_not_found`, `number_not_in_quote`, `not_supported`, `unsupported_field`, `forbidden_pattern`, `claim_too_long`, `invalid`. `size_cap` is gone.
- `flagged` keys: `partial_support`, `unchecked`, `other_region`, `other_entity`, `duplicate_source`, `number_widened`.
- Gap reasons: `not_found`, `budget_stop`, `provider_down`, `not_run`, `not_worthwhile`, `conflict_unresolved`, `user_cancelled`.
- The docs give no prose definitions of `validation`, `flagged` or `profile` beyond the shape above.

**Evidence ladder (computed by code):**

| Value | Rule |
|---|---|
| `official` | At least 1 source of class `official` or `help` that is the origin of the fact, and the check said `supported`. |
| `corroborated` | At least 2 content-independent organizations (RV-19), none of class forum, blog or seo, and `supported`. |
| `single_source` | One source, `supported`. |
| `unverified` | Check was `partial` or `unchecked`, or the item is flagged `other_region`, `other_entity` or `number_widened` with no second source. |

**`lane_quality`:**
- `strong`: 80% or more of the lane's required slots filled AND 60% or more of its items official or corroborated.
- `partial`: 50% or more of slots filled.
- `thin`: some items, under 50% of slots.
- `none`: no item.

**`bn_coverage`:**
- `researched`: at least one official or corroborated item maps to the need via `data/lanes.yaml`.
- `single_source_only`: only single_source items.
- `defaulted_only`: no item, so Spec proposes the module default.
- `gap`: the need had topics and all were `not_found` or not run.

**§9.3 compact view:**
- `GET .../latest?view=compact` is a deterministic projection, not a stored file.
- It holds items, norms, gaps, confirm, suggestions and defaults.
- `sources{}` is a table keyed by id, and items hold ids.
- `use_note` appears only when it differs from the kind default.
- Every main-store item has a Brief link or `NEW`.
- `pack(need_card)` is pure code with no embeddings and no model. Selection is by structure.

**Other v5 rules:**
- **Ordered lists** (`steps`, `transitions`) are never concatenated. Keep the richest list and store the others as `variants`.
- **Norm levels:** `core` at share ≥0.6, `common` at ≥0.3, `emerging` (recent, rising, seen in at most 2), `limited_evidence` under 3 sources.
- **Integration norms:** one per (need, provider), counting only independent third-party mentions. The provider's own docs are `official` and do not count.
- **Excluded from norms:** items flagged `other_region` or `other_entity`.
- **Required slot for high impact:** official, or 2 independent organizations, else flagged `single-source`.

## 13 §10 events and snapshot; §10.1 standalone app
**Event envelope:** `id`, `seq`, `ts`, `run_id`, `type`, `target_id`, `round`, `phase`, `label`, `detail`, and `counters` with:
- `searches_used`
- `searches_ceiling`
- `pages_read`
- `facts_verified`
- `targets_done`
- `targets_total`
- `elapsed_s`
- `eta_s`

**Event types:**
- `run.started`
- `plan.ready`
- `target.started`, `target.covered`, `target.not_found`, `target.cancelled`
- `query.issued`
- `search.results`
- `page.reading`, `page.read`, `page.skipped` (reasons `no_content`, `paywall`, `blocked`)
- `fact.found`, `fact.verified`, `fact.rejected`
- `entity.discovered`
- `round.completed`
- `gap.detected`
- `warning`
- `run.finished`, `run.skipped`, `run.failed`

**v5 additions:**
- `plan.ready` carries `slots_required` per target and in total.
- `fact.*` events carry a stable `fact_id`.
- The snapshot keeps a `facts` list instead of the last 60 events.
- `run.finished` carries stage timings and cost by stage.
- New endpoints:
  - `GET /research/runs/{id}/report`
  - `GET /research/runs/{id}/spot-check?n=60&amp;seed=&lt;int&gt;`
  - `GET /research/programs/{id}/latest?view=compact`
  - `GET /research/programs/{id}/decisions`
- D-130's `apply_decisions=1` does not exist, so implement it or correct D-130.
- Events carry no page text or quotes (RV-17).

**§10.1 standalone app:**
- Command and address: `python -m mde_research_agent` on `127.0.0.1:8120`. One process and one origin, with a static UI in `agents/research/ui/` and no build step.
- No `--memory`, no mock banner, no replay mode. If the switches or keys are missing, the app starts but refuses runs and says what to set.
- Inputs folder: `docs/compass/research-inputs/`, overridable with `RESEARCH_INPUTS_DIR`. Each file is listed with its computed size (tiny, small, medium, big) and a zero-cost preview.
- Runs: one at a time with a queue. The store is file-backed by default, PostgreSQL optional, and both must pass the same contract tests.
- Money: no per-run cap. The total is USD 5.00, with a tripwire at 4.50 (the run in progress finishes, nothing new starts) and a hard stop at 5.00 (`partial`).
- Prompt 06 adds:
  - the data folder `agents/research/.runtime/`
  - the origin check on state-changing requests
  - a 25 MB file limit
  - a 10 s auto-refresh
  - the UI budgets (first paint under 1 s, JavaScript under about 100 KB gzipped)
  - `scripts/research_app` for start, stop, status, logs and restart

## 13 §11 rules R21 to R27
- **R21:** Spec may make a finding a Default only if its evidence is official or corroborated. A single_source finding becomes Open or goes on `confirm`. An unverified finding never enters a decision.
- **R22:** Numbers and legal facts are always on `confirm` and shown at G2.
- **R23:** Spec records `claims_used`, and Verify's drift check compares them to the quotes.
- **R24:** If `lane_quality` is thin or none, Spec writes "assumed" from its module library and never invents a source, number, provider or quote.
- **R25:** Prevalence reads "mentioned in n of N comparable pages", not market share. Fewer than 3 sources is `limited_evidence`.
- **R26:** No silent loss. Findings go to the appendix with a reason, and every drop has a counted reason.
- **R27:** No attribute the page does not state. Types, flags, enums, steps and dates come from the passage or page metadata, else null.

## 13 §12 RS-35 to RS-47
- **RS-35:** A number or date is not in the quote. Widen to its sentence, else reject `number_not_in_quote`.
- **RS-36:** Page about another country or company. Flag `other_region` or `other_entity`: no corroboration, no prevalence, never Default, kept.
- **RS-37:** Same text on several domains counts as one source (RV-19).
- **RS-38:** The checker rejects the only item filling a required slot. The slot reopens before the audit and a follow-up runs.
- **RS-39:** Tiny app. Light mode, at most about 8 searches, gaps `not_worthwhile`.
- **RS-40:** The primary provider errors or is rate-limited. Wait, then fail over for that topic, with a `warning` event. If both fail, `provider_down` and `partial`.
- **RS-41:** JavaScript-only page, scanned PDF, wall text. `page.skipped` with a reason. Use provider text if it passes the wall check. Never extract a high-impact claim from a snippet. Rendered fetch is P2.
- **RS-42:** Store exceeds `store_tokens`. Move the lowest-ranked findings to the appendix with a reason. If the protected set alone exceeds it, warn and the store grows.
- **RS-43:** Single process is assumed (no lease). Startup recovery may wrongly mark another process's live run `interrupted`.
- **RS-44:** The extractor adds an unstated type, flag or enum. Nulled or dropped, counted `unsupported_field`.
- **RS-45:** The checker call fails or gives no verdict. The item is `unverified` and flagged `unchecked`, counted, never dropped silently.
- **RS-46:** Claim over the word limit. Cut only at a sentence end, else reject `claim_too_long`.
- **RS-47:** No events for 10 minutes (default `stale_after_s` 600). Mark `failed: stalled` with a terminal event.

## 13 §13 RV-18 to RV-23
- **RV-18:** Every number, amount, percentage and date in `claim` and numeric `data` occurs in the quote (normalized). Otherwise widen to the sentence, else reject.
- **RV-19:** Independence by content. Near-duplicate passages (5-word shingle overlap ≥0.8) count once in `independent_domains`, corroboration and norms. Flag `duplicate_source`.
- **RV-20:** `about_region` and `about_entity` agree with the plan's place and the topic's anchor. Otherwise flag `other_region` or `other_entity`, exclude from corroboration, norms and Default, and keep as `unverified`.
- **RV-21:** Every verified item has a verdict or the `unchecked` flag. No unsupported field remains in `data`. Checks finish before the audit. A mismatch is a bug.
- **RV-22:** Main-store tokens are at most `store_tokens` unless the protected set alone exceeds it. Every appendix item has a reason. Count is conserved (verified atoms = items + merged). Enforced. A mismatch is a bug and the commit is marked `partial`.
- **RV-23:** `evidence`, `lane_quality` and `bn_coverage` recomputed from the items equal the stored values.

**Enforcement:** Per prompt RC-1, RV-09 and RV-22 are enforced. Per RC-3, RV-05 is enforced. Doc 13 §5.1 also says RV-09 and RV-05 are enforced.

## 14 §3 metric definitions
- **Extraction quote pass rate:** quotes found on the first try over all extractions, also reported after the retry. Target ≥90%. The built metric re-checks passed atoms, so it is always about 100%.
- **Number-check rejection rate:** `number_not_in_quote` rejections over all extractions. Target under 3%.
- **Fetch success:** selected pages that returned text over selected pages, by failure reason. Target ≥70%.
- **Searches contributing:** searches that produced a kept item over all searches. Target ≥60%.
- **Required slots filled:** filled over required, per job and in total. Target ≥70%. Design and screens are reported separately.
- **Appendix share:** appendix items over all items. If above 30% at standard or 60% at deep, compact the view before raising the limit.
- **Lane quality:** the value per lane.
- **Checker rates:** shares of `supported`, `partial`, `not_supported` and `unchecked`. Target `not_supported` under 10%. A rate under 1% rejections is a rubber-stamp signal.
- **Stage timings and cost:** per stage and per call kind.
- **Merge rate:** atoms over items and norm keys over norms. If unrelated things are merged, tighten the Jaccard 0.6 threshold.

## 14 §4 spot-check
- **Sample:** 60 items, 20 from each of Quick, Standard and Thorough, drawn by a fixed seed so it is repeatable.
- **Strata per run, 8 + 6 + 3 + 3 = 20:**
  - 8 high-impact items (fees, limits, rules, formats)
  - 6 service or integration facts
  - 3 typed-data items (entity fields, screen sections)
  - 3 ordinary features, flows or norms
- **No numeric seed is given anywhere.** The only hint is `?n=60&amp;seed=&lt;int&gt;`, and the UI shows the seed.
- **No fallback is given** for a stratum with too few items.
- **Verdict codes:** `ok`, `not relevant`, `claim not in quote`, `wrong or outdated`, `wrong place or company`, `invented detail`. UI keys 1 to 6.
- **Pass:**
  - `ok` plus `not relevant` at 95% or more;
  - `ok` alone at 90% or more;
  - no `invented detail` on a high-impact item and fewer than 3 overall.
- **A single fabricated fact is a blocker** (tighten RC-3 grounding, redo).
- **If it fails:** the failing job or kind loses Default eligibility until fixed, then redo with 30 new items.

## 14 §5 planted-error test (inside PG-3)
- **Sample:** 40 clean verified items, stratified like §4, with a fixed seed (no value given). Five corruption kinds give 200 corrupted variants, plus the 40 clean items:
  - number changed
  - entity swapped
  - condition flipped (add or remove "not", "only if", "excl. tax")
  - region swapped (currency, country or authority)
  - unsupported attribute added
- **Report separately** what the code rules catch (RV-18, RV-20, field support) and what the checker catches on what code let through.
- **Pass:** at least 90% caught overall, at least 80% per kind, and at most 5% of clean items wrongly rejected.
- **If the catch rate is under 90%:** a second vendor is required (OI-23).

## 14 §6 consumer probe (PG-5)
- **Stand-in:** Luna stands in for Spec, on recorded output, with no web calls.
- **Five tasks (data files):**
  - first-release feature list
  - entities with fields, statuses and numeric rules
  - integration options with auth, fees and limits
  - the sections and states of 5 key screens
  - sample data and formats
- **Citations:** each answer must cite finding ids or write `ASSUMED`.
- **Arms:** A is the Brief only. B is the Brief plus the research (the whole store if it fits, else a pack). For a deep run, B is run both ways (PG-6).
- **Judge counts:** supported, `ASSUMED`, invented, and BN coverage reached. A human reads 10 "invented" verdicts.
- **Pass:** B has more supported statements and fewer `ASSUMED`, and invented is not higher than A.
- **PG-6:** the pack must score within 5% of the full store. If the whole store is clearly better at 60k or 80k, raise the threshold and `store_tokens` together. Skip this gate if the store is far below 40k.

## 14 §8 failure drills (PG-7)
- Primary provider errors → fail over for that topic, `warning` event.
- Both providers fail → `provider_down`, `partial`, no exception to the caller.
- JavaScript-only page, scanned PDF or cookie wall → `page.skipped` with a reason, no high-impact claim from a snippet.
- Injected instructions → ignored and counted, no tool, URL or action field in any output.
- Checker rejects the only item of a required slot → the slot reopens and a follow-up runs.
- Checker call fails → items `unverified`, flagged `unchecked`.
- Store exceeds `store_tokens` → lowest-ranked to the appendix with a reason, counts conserved.
- Time limit hit during checks → checks and assembly stop with the run, `partial` with a `budget_stop` warning.
- Run killed mid-way → after the stale period, `failed: stalled` with a terminal event.
- Tiny app → light mode, about 8 searches at most, `complete`.
- Same Brief with a different tier value → identical result.

USD 5.00 total, tripwire 4.50, hard stop 5.00, with no per-run or call-count cap. Within 1.3 times the stage estimate, go on. Above it, stop and re-plan.

| B | 0.72 | 0.90 |
| C | 1.45 | 1.70 |

## 1. Shared live-spend ledger

**Storage**
- The ledger is `data/.budget/ledger.jsonl`, with `ledger.jsonl.guard` and `ledger.jsonl.lock` beside it.
- `/data/` is gitignored (`.gitignore`), so the ledger and its history are not versioned.
- Right now it holds 3626 lines (1.28 MB). The guard records `spent_micros` 2357155 (USD 2.36 at last write) and the first-line sha256.
- The path comes from the `LLM_BUDGET_LEDGER` env var, default `data/.budget/ledger.jsonl` (`packages/core/src/mde_core/settings.py:40`, `.env.example:8`).
- A relative path is anchored to the MAIN repo root by `mde_core.paths.anchor_to_main_repo` (`paths.py:69`), so worktrees share one ledger.
- `budget_cli --ledger PATH` overrides the path for reads and for `init`. The gateway factory itself has no flag, only the env var (`factory.py:113-122`).

**Format**
- JSONL, one `LedgerEvent` per line (`ledger.py:75-89`).
- Fields: `event` (reserve, commit or release), `ts`, `reservation_id`, `scopes`, `call_id`, `spec_id`, `model`, `mode`, `worst_case_micros`, `usage` and `cost_micros`. The model is `extra="forbid"`.
- There is no period or epoch field. Only scopes group spend.

**How spend is computed**
- `LedgerState` folds every line from the start (`ledger.py:115-195`). Spent is the sum of commit `cost_micros`, split by scope. Open reservations sit in "reserved".
- Reservations older than the TTL (600 s) count as SPENT at their worst case (`ledger.py:159`).

**Caps and tripwires** (`budget.py`)
- `HARD_CAP_USD` is 8.00 (line 48) and `HARD_EFFECTIVE_CAP_USD` is 7.60 (line 49).
- `BudgetConfig.cap_micros` is `LLM_BUDGET_USD`, clamped to 8.00 with a warning (lines 76-86). The `Settings` default is 6.00.
- `effective_cap_micros` is min(cap × 0.95, 7.60) (lines 92-98).
- `tripwire()` (lines 118-126) is based on spent+reserved over `cap_micros`, not the effective cap. At ≥ 0.50 it warns. At ≥ 0.80 only purposes starting `must-run` pass. At ≥ 0.95 everything is refused.
- With cap 8.00 those thresholds are USD 4.00, 6.40 and 7.60.
- `reserve()` is atomic inside the ledger lock (lines 271-375). It refuses if committed + worst &gt; effective cap, globally or in any scope.
- Scope caps (`budget.yaml`, `_DEFAULT_SCOPE_CAPS_USD`) are session 1.00, research 0.30, eval 1.50 and test 0.30. `scope_overrides_usd` is empty. An unknown scope kind is refused (lines 100-109).
- `commit` books the worst case if the usage is unpriceable (lines 377-412).
- `status()` returns spent, reserved and by_scope (lines 485-495).

**`mde budget`**
- `just budget` runs `python -m mde_llm_gateway.budget_cli` (`justfile:110`).
- It is read-only and never takes the lock. `FileLedger.peek()` feeds `build_report`, which prints spent, % of cap, % of effective cap, tripwire, by scope and the integrity problem (`budget_cli.py:41-173`).
- There is a `--scope` prefix filter and `--json`.

**Archiving and restarting at 0.00**
- The guard makes a plain move or delete fail closed. The ledger is deleted or truncated, so the next reserve raises `LedgerIntegrityError` (`ledger.py:393-419`).
- A reset needs one of these, after reconciling with the provider dashboard:
  - `python -m mde_llm_gateway.budget_cli init --force`, or
  - `MDE_BUDGET_LEDGER_RESET=1`, which `factory.py:122` reads (`ENV_LEDGER_RESET`).
- The CLI's `init --force` (`budget_cli.py:142-156`) calls `reset_guard()`, which for a missing ledger removes the guard (`ledger.py:488-510`). It then `touch`es a new empty ledger.
- Sequence that keeps history:
  1. Move `data/.budget/ledger.jsonl`, `.guard` and `.lock` to e.g. `data/.budget/archive/&lt;date&gt;/`.
  2. Run `init --force`.
- Alternatively, point `LLM_BUDGET_LEDGER` at a new file such as `data/.budget/ledger-2026-11-11.jsonl` and leave the old one untouched. That needs no guard reset, because a fresh path creates its own guard.
- Risks:
  - Spent resets to 0 while the project total in CLAUDE.md rule 3 (the 8.00 ceiling) is not tracked across ledgers. Roughly USD 2.36 is already spent. The 8.00 cap would apply only to new spend.
  - Anything that hardcodes the ledger path breaks. `evals/runners/live_batch.py`, `tests/live/test_smoke.py` and `tests/security/test_repo_hygiene.py` mention "ledger". I did not check whether they use `settings.llm_budget_ledger` or a literal path.
  - `LLM_BUDGET_USD` is only read from settings. Setting it to 8.00 is an env or `.env` change that I did not inspect.

**Research cost booking** (`agents/research/src/mde_research_agent/engine/spend.py`)
- `GuardSpendBook.__call__` (lines 53-76) does `guard.reserve((f"{RUN_SCOPE_KIND}:{run_id}",), micros, purpose="research.search", spec_id=f"rsch.search.{provider}", model=provider, mode="live")`. It then calls `guard.commit_cost(res, micros)`.
- Model calls book under `rsch.*` spec ids.
- `research_spent_micros(ledger, prefix="rsch.")` (lines 22-50) re-reads the JSONL and sums commit `cost_micros` where the reservation's `spec_id` starts with `rsch.`. It has no period or epoch filter, so a new ledger file effectively resets it too.
- It is used at `service.py:134` (`_remaining_budget`, against `RESEARCH_LIVE_BUDGET_USD`, default 4.00, and the per-run cap `RESEARCH_RUN_CAP_USD` 2.50) and at `live_runs.py:370`.
- The scope kind is `RUN_SCOPE_KIND` from `config.py`. Its value must be in `scope_kinds`, since `budget.yaml` has `research: 0.30` per scope. I did not verify the value.

## 2. Hook (`.claude/hooks/[live-mode rule phrase].py`, `.claude/settings.json`)

**How it matches**
- It registers as a PreToolUse hook for the `Bash` and `PowerShell` matchers (`settings.json`).
- It reads `tool_input.command`, normalises it (`normalise`, lines ~85-110), then runs `is_live_command` (lines ~140-150). It exits 0 if `[live-mode rule phrase]=1` is in the environment.
- The patterns:
  - `_ASSIGN`, `_ARGS`, `_JUST_LIVE`: LLM_MODE=live and `just live-*`.
  - `_LIVE_RUNNER`: `evals/runners/live_batch`.
  - `_RESEARCH_LIVE = r"mde_research_agent[./\\]+live_[a-z]"`, checked per `;&amp;|` segment. It is blocked unless the same segment has `--dry-run`, `--mock` or `--replay` (`_runs_research_live`).
- The hook sees command text only.

**Current coverage for the research app**
- `python -m mde_research_agent` (the app, no flags) does NOT match `_RESEARCH_LIVE`, because the pattern needs `.live_`. It is allowed.
- A `--memory --port 8120` form is already an explicit "False" case (`tests/security/test_hardening_static.py:335`).
- `pytest agents/research ...` is allowed (line 337), as are `grep ... live_runs`, `.run --export x.json --mock` and `.live_smoke --dry-run --offline`.
- Neither the research eval commands nor `mde_research_agent.live_*` run via a wrapper are covered. The only named live modules are `live_bakeoff`, `live_runs` and `live_smoke` (the `live_smoke` module is not in the research src listing: only `live_bakeoff.py` and `live_runs.py` exist).

**Tests** (`tests/security/test_hardening_static.py`)
- True and False command cases are in the `CASES` list (lines ~230-345), with research cases at lines 298-306 (blocked) and 331-338 (allowed).
- `test_guard_hook_matchers` (line 356) is parametrised over `CASES`.
- `test_guard_hook_process_exit_codes` runs for both tools (line 371).
- `test_guard_hook_errors_never_block` is at line 401.
- `test_guard_hook_is_registered_for_both_shell_tools` is at line 405.

**What adding entry points would need**
- A new CLI module such as `mde_research_agent.eval` or a `just research-eval` recipe is not caught by `_RESEARCH_LIVE` unless its module name starts with `live_`.
- If eval commands should be gated, extend the regex or name the module `live_eval` and add cases to `CASES`.
- `pytest` stays unaffected as long as new patterns require `mde_research_agent[./\\]+&lt;name&gt;` and do not match plain `pytest agents/research`.
- Risk: the scan operates on the normalised text. `python -m mde_research_agent` followed by a path containing `live_` would only match with `.` or `/` directly between the package name and the module.

## 3. Layout rules and docs checks

**GLOBAL_RULES B** (`docs/implementation/GLOBAL_RULES.md:18-25`)
- Line 20: `agents/&lt;name&gt;/` is self-contained. It lists prompts, schemas, engine, store, API router and `create_app()`, tests, evals and README, plus build prompts under `docs/`. It does not list a UI.
- Line 21: `apps/web/` is the global front end (static HTML, CSS and vanilla JS now, React later), "never inside an agent folder".
- Line 24: agents import shared packages only. The front end talks to agents only via HTTP and the shared JSON Schemas.
- Line 25: `apps/web/console/agents/&lt;name&gt;/` holds the screens. The agent's dev server serves static files from `MDE_WEB_DIR` (default `apps/web/console`).
- So an `agents/research/ui/` folder with static html, js and css contradicts line 21 in text. Nothing enforces it mechanically.
- I found no test that checks the layout rule.
- The Research server today has no static mount. Only Compass mounts `StaticFiles` (`agents/compass/src/mde_compass/app.py:20,30-60`, `web_dir()`).

**`.importlinter`**
- `mde_research_agent` appears in the layers contract (line 26, same layer as `mde_compass`) and in the openai and agent-frameworks contracts (lines 66 and 90).
- Contract `research-agent-isolated` (lines 142-160) forbids imports of every other first-party package from `mde_research_agent`. Static files are not Python, so they are not affected.
- A new Python module that serves them would need to import only fastapi/starlette, which are third-party and allowed.

**Justfile**
- `research-dev` (line 55) runs `python -m mde_research_agent --port 8120` and `research-dev-memory` (line 58) adds `--memory`.
- Also `research-migrate`, `research-test` and `research-test-int`.

**`.gitignore`**
- `/data/` and `evals/reports/` are ignored, as are `.claude/settings.local.json`, `.claude/worktrees/`, `.env` and `.env.*` (except `.env.example`).
- A runtime PID or log directory under `/data/` is already ignored.

**`.md` check** (`scripts/docs_check.py:3,18,49`)
- `.md` files are allowed only as README.md, CLAUDE.md or AGENTS.md at any depth, under `.claude/` and under `docs/`.
- A `.md` in `agents/research/` that is not named README, CLAUDE or AGENTS would fail docs-check.
- The check scans git ls-files plus untracked, non-ignored files.
